### Ereignis 2: Runde 1

- Reviewer: `claude`
- Freigabe: `NO`
- Validierungsbindung: `validation-f321643cbad9`
- Testdateien: `tests/test_artifact_bridge.py`, `tests/test_artifact_store.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py`, `tests/test_validation_matrix.py`
- Prüfdimensionen: Checked dimensions — (1) ValidationCommandSpec/ValidationAttestation invariants (argv-vs-legacy_shell exclusivity, control-character rejection, lossless display-order equality with expected_commands — sound); (2) ArtifactBridge.append idempotency/dedup and its documented "durable-but-reported-failed" recovery path re-scanning &#96;load_chain()&#96; before re-raising (sound, matches closed C-03 contract, verified against &#96;test_bridge_is_idempotent_before_creating_volatile_metadata&#96; and &#96;test_bridge_rejects_idempotency_key_with_different_meaning&#96;); (3) &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96; fail-closed wrapper placement relative to state/history mutation at each call site (gate decision, Codex contract, review contract, diagnostics, validation request/attestation) — persistence consistently precedes the corresponding in-memory history update, so a structured-store failure blocks the step rather than silently diverging state-v3 from the structured mirror, at the cost of re-running expensive validation on structured-store hiccups (accepted, pre-mortemed risk, not re-raised here); (4) &#96;attestation_payload()&#96;'s &#96;records_by_display&#96; dict keyed by command display string — correct for the common case but silently collapses to the *last* matching record if two &#96;command_specs&#96; ever share an identical display string, mis-attributing exit code/outcome; left as a documented residual risk rather than a new finding because a matrix run producing two textually identical commands is not demonstrated as reachable from current callers and the mis-projection would only corrupt the mirrored audit view, not the state-v3 decision path that remains authoritative until Slice 06 cutover.
- Größtes Restrisiko: Largest residual risk: exactly the C-04 mechanism generalized — any future &#96;_persist_structured_baseline&#96;/bridge call site that reuses an idempotency key while allowing payload content to vary across repeated invocations will hard-crash &#96;bind_work_unit&#96;/the workflow step instead of failing closed with a clear diagnostic.
- Realistische Bruchbedingung: Realistic break condition: a Slice-03-bound run whose first implementation round is reviewed and rejected, triggering a second Codex round under the same &#96;work_unit_id&#96;; the very next &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call raises &#96;ArtifactBridgeError&#96; and halts the run with no actionable message tying the failure back to the round-number/idempotency-key mismatch.
- Eigene Findings: `C-01`, `C-02`, `C-03`, `C-04`

### Ereignis 4: Runde 2

- Reviewer: `claude`
- Freigabe: `YES`
- Validierungsbindung: `validation-43392b3ca365`
- Testdateien: `tests/test_artifact_bridge.py`, `tests/test_artifact_store.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py`, `tests/test_validation_matrix.py`
- Prüfdimensionen: Checked dimensions — (1) work-unit idempotency-key/round-number fix directly re-derived from source and cross-checked against both the new transition test and the pre-existing same-round idempotency test; (2) confirmed no other &#96;_persist_structured_baseline&#96; branch (task-contract, approved-plan) embeds a per-round-varying field under a round-invariant key, so the fixed mechanism is the only instance of the pattern; (3) scope/allowlist conformance — every touched path (artifact_bridge.py, contracts.py, orchestrator.py, validation_matrix.py, workflow.py, artifact_store.py docstring, and the five listed test files) is inside the exact current slice allowlist, no UNEXPECTED-PATH; (4) fingerprint binding — validation-43392b3ca365 matches CURRENT FINGERPRINT exactly, full suite 744/0
- Größtes Restrisiko: Largest residual risk: &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96; and the commit-binding bridge call remain direct, unwrapped driver calls (not routed through &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96;'s fail-closed try/except), so any future semantic-mismatch bug in those specific call sites would still surface as a raw &#96;ArtifactBridgeError&#96;/&#96;WorkflowExecutionError&#96; out of &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96;/commit binding rather than a uniformly wrapped diagnostic
- Realistische Bruchbedingung: Realistic break condition: a later slice adds another field to &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96; or the plan/task payloads that legitimately varies within a single logical_id/idempotency_key scope without updating the key accordingly, reintroducing the same class of hard crash on resume.
- Eigene Findings: `C-01`, `C-02`, `C-03`, `C-04`

- Prüfdimensionen: checked dimensions = symmetric fail-closed verification for every mirrorable fact class in resolve_resume_state() (task, plan, work-unit/round, gate decision, finding transition, validation attestation, quota pause, commit binding, workflow completion); quota_facts/quota_records tuple-key alignment against InvocationFailureRecord and QuotaPausePayload field shapes; non-regression of the four previously-symmetric checks (untouched this round); isolation of the new regression test from the wait_automatically=True/automatic-resume path; confirmation no other slice-04 file changed in this delta.
- Größtes Restrisiko: largest residual risk = &#96;quota_facts&#96; is filtered only on &#96;diff_fingerprint is not None and resume_at_utc is not None&#96;, independent of the &#96;wait_automatically&#96;/automatic-resume flag; if a future caller sets both fields for a manually-gated (non-automatic) quota-adjacent wait that is not by design backed by a persisted &#96;QuotaPausePayload&#96; chain record, this now-symmetric check would incorrectly halt resume on an otherwise legitimate mirror state — the &#96;QuotaPausePayload&#96;-emission contract itself lives outside this slice's diff and could not be directly inspected here.
- Realistische Bruchbedingung: realistic break condition = a later slice (5/6 cutover/hardening work) extends &#96;InvocationFailureRecord&#96; usage to a manual or non-automatic wait scenario that legitimately sets &#96;diff_fingerprint&#96;/&#96;resume_at_utc&#96; without emitting a matching chain record, causing every resume of that run to raise &#96;ArtifactResumeError&#96; even though the mirror is legitimate, and this is unguarded because the only existing coverage exercises the &#96;wait_automatically=True&#96; path.
- Eigene Findings: `C-01`, `C-02`, `C-03`, `C-04`, `C-05`, `C-06`

### Ereignis 2: Runde 1

- Reviewer: `claude`
- Freigabe: `YES`
- Validierungsbindung: `validation-a834bf158114`
- Testdateien: `tests/test_structured_artifact_regressions.py`, `tests/test_workflow.py`
- Prüfdimensionen: correctness of the control-flow reorder, resume/idempotency behavior across chain-vs-mirror agreement, non-stop verdict path non-regression, exception-safety of the new call ordering, scope/allowlist conformance
- Größtes Restrisiko: largest residual risk: the fix is positional/manual — nothing structurally forces every future early-return branch inside &#96;_run_review()&#96; to call &#96;_record_review()&#96; first, so the same class of chain-ahead-of-mirror bug can reappear if a new terminal reviewer verdict type is added later without care
- Realistische Bruchbedingung: break condition: a future change adds a new reviewer verdict/terminal branch to &#96;_run_review()&#96; that returns and checkpoints before reaching &#96;_record_review()&#96;, silently reopening a chain/mirror divergence that no existing test would catch since coverage is scoped to the &#96;stopped&#96; and approval/denial branches present today.
- Eigene Findings: `C-01`, `C-02`, `C-03`, `C-04`, `C-05`, `C-06`, `C-07`, `C-08`, `C-09`, `C-10`, `C-11`, `C-12`, `C-13`

- Realistische Bruchbedingung: Break condition: a future correction round bundles an actual functional defect alongside a similarly undocumented, untracked change, and because report review historically treats "adjacent regressions stay green" as sufficient cover for new behavior, a real bug ships without ever being individually attributed to a finding ID or acceptance test.

- Prüfdimensionen: Checked correctness of the narrowed completion-check gate against its own regression test and the 4 previously-broken production tests; symmetry with the C-18 lifecycle-gating precedent; fail-closed retention for the genuine terminal-completion case; absence of any other C-* finding still open (C-01–C-19 all previously closed and re-verified, C-20 closed above)
- Größtes Restrisiko: Largest residual risk: the fix's correctness depends on &#96;current_work_unit.kind&#96; never reverting away from &#96;FINAL_REVIEW&#96;/&#96;PLAN&#96; after genuine terminal completion, which is asserted by the passing suite but not independently proven by a dedicated adversarial test for that specific invariant
- Realistische Bruchbedingung: Realistic break condition: a future workflow-step extension (e.g., a post-FINAL_REVIEW cleanup/archival work unit) that transitions &#96;current_work_unit&#96; away from &#96;FINAL_REVIEW&#96; while &#96;current_step&#96; remains &#96;COMPLETED&#96;, causing a truncated/mismatched-restore mirror at that later work-unit kind to silently resume without a chain &#96;WorkflowCompletionPayload&#96;, reopening a narrower variant of C-19/C-20.
- Eigene Findings: `C-01`, `C-02`, `C-03`, `C-04`, `C-05`, `C-06`, `C-07`, `C-08`, `C-09`, `C-10`, `C-11`, `C-12`, `C-13`, `C-14`, `C-15`, `C-16`, `C-17`, `C-18`, `C-19`, `C-20`

### Ereignis 6: Runde 3

- Reviewer: `claude`
- Freigabe: `YES`
- Validierungsbindung: `validation-3ebb90977e13`
- Testdateien: `tests/test_artifact_migration.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py`, `tests/test_workflow.py`
- Prüfdimensionen: correctness of the AUTH-misclassification recovery predicate and its non-bypass of downstream contract validation; idempotency-key stability of recovered vs. normal reviewer invocations; correctness and test-proven behavior of the Claude-review-rewind-on-acknowledged-diff-change fix including the reviewer call-order and fingerprint-binding assertions; completeness of the Antigravity-step-to-Claude-step rewind map against all three review kinds; absence of interaction with the FINAL_REVIEW/PLAN terminal completion-check invariant from C-19/C-20; path/branch/scope conformance
- Größtes Restrisiko: Largest residual risk: the AUTH-recovery predicate trusts any provider_text shaped like a complete reviewer contract for the expected role without independently correlating it to the specific failed invocation, relying on the existing single-process local-CLI trust boundary rather than a stronger binding
- Realistische Bruchbedingung: Realistic break condition: a future change to the agent adapter layer that buffers or retries provider output across invocations (e.g., connection-pooling or output caching) could let a stale prior successful reviewer contract for the same role leak into a later invocation's AUTH-error provider_text, causing this recovery path to silently accept a genuinely stale review instead of the current diff's review.
- Eigene Findings: `C-01`, `C-02`, `C-03`, `C-04`, `C-05`, `C-06`, `C-07`, `C-08`, `C-09`, `C-10`, `C-11`, `C-12`, `C-13`, `C-14`, `C-15`, `C-16`, `C-17`, `C-18`, `C-19`, `C-20`

### Ereignis 2: Runde 1

- Reviewer: `claude`
- Freigabe: `YES`
- Validierungsbindung: `validation-60d8c851c799`
- Testdateien: `tests/test_artifact_bridge.py`, `tests/test_artifact_migration.py`, `tests/test_artifact_models.py`, `tests/test_artifact_projection.py`, `tests/test_artifact_store.py`, `tests/test_audit_trail.py`, `tests/test_inbox_watcher.py`, `tests/test_language_consistency.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator_watch_cli.py`, `tests/test_state_io.py`, `tests/test_structured_artifact_regressions.py`, `tests/test_validation_matrix.py`, `tests/test_workflow.py`, `tests/test_workflow_state.py`
- Prüfdimensionen: (1) architecture drift: BindingPayload writer-side authorization (commit_slice()/persist_implementation_handoff() in src/orchestrator.py) always derives attestation_id/approval_ids from a live scan of the append-only chain matching the exact request fingerprint before any commit, so every binding written today is provenance-correct at write time; (2) interface consistency: all 15 record families in schemas/orchestrator-artifact-v1.schema.json and src/artifact_models.py remain closed (additionalProperties:false) and round-trip losslessly through canonical JSON (tests/test_artifact_models.py); (3) dead transition states: ResumeCheckPayload has a model and reader in resolve_resume_state() but no production writer — confirmed dead but inert, since resolve_resume_state() only cross-checks a predecessor binding when one is present and never requires one to exist; (4) documentation synchronization: artifact_bridge.py's module docstring ("State-v3 remains authoritative until the explicit cutover") is stale relative to the Slice-06 source-of-truth cutover, but no runtime code or test asserts that sentence, so it is inert prose drift; (5) requirements/acceptance: independently re-derived every C-01–C-20 fix against current source (round-scoped WorkUnitPayload idempotency keys, symmetric chain↔mirror checks for gate decisions/finding transitions/quota pauses/workflow completion, execution-mode-gated plan-binding resume, production QuotaPausePayload/CorrectionWorkUnitPayload writers, stop-review mirroring before checkpoint, immutable approved-plan-commit binding) — every fix is present in the diff and matches its closure rationale; the bound attestation validation-60d8c851c799 (787 passed / 0 failed) matches CURRENT FINGERPRINT 60d8c851c799 exactly and no C-* finding remains open.
- Größtes Restrisiko: Largest residual risk: the append-only store validates BindingPayload.attestation_id/approval_ids only as well-formed identifiers (regex-shaped), not as references that resolve to an existing, type- and fingerprint-matching record within the same chain; resolve_resume_state()'s commit-binding check likewise compares only the binding's &#96;target&#96; commit hash, not its attestation/approval provenance, and assert_structured_decision_context() compares reviewer decisions as an unordered Counter rather than an ordered sequence — safe today only because commit_slice() and persist_implementation_handoff() are the sole production call sites that ever construct a BindingPayload, and both always derive attestation_id/approval_ids from a live re-scan of the chain immediately before writing.
- Realistische Bruchbedingung: Realistic break condition: a future workflow extension adds another call site that appends a BindingPayload without routing through that same live-chain-scan pattern (or a hand-edited/restored record file carries a schema-valid but fabricated attestation_id/approval_ids), and neither artifact_store's scan-time invariants nor resolve_resume_state()'s checks would detect the fabricated provenance, since no invariant in artifact_store.py or artifact_migration.py verifies that a binding's attestation_id/approval_ids actually resolve to matching chain records — silently weakening the audit trail's evidentiary guarantee while the full test suite keeps passing.
- Eigene Findings: `C-01`, `C-02`, `C-03`, `C-04`, `C-05`, `C-06`, `C-07`, `C-08`, `C-09`, `C-10`, `C-11`, `C-12`, `C-13`, `C-14`, `C-15`, `C-16`, `C-17`, `C-18`, `C-19`, `C-20`

### Ereignis 4: Runde 2

- Reviewer: `claude`
- Freigabe: `YES`
- Validierungsbindung: `validation-2e0d30731579`
- Testdateien: `tests/test_artifact_migration.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py`
- Prüfdimensionen: correctness of the C-21 referential-integrity check (both fabricated-record and denied-verdict paths verified fail-closed), non-regression of the C-19 completion check against the newly-required real attestation/approval records, and correctness/completeness of the C-22 AUTH-vs-QUOTA classification boundary at both the unit (recovery-gate) and integration (full quota-wait/persist) level
- Größtes Restrisiko: Largest residual risk: the append-only store still validates &#96;BindingPayload.approval_ids&#96; membership only against &#96;ReviewPayload.work_unit_id&#96;-agnostic fingerprint+verdict matching, so a review record for a different work unit that happens to share the exact same diff fingerprint could theoretically authorize an unrelated binding; this is a pre-existing, narrow, fingerprint-collision-bound risk not reopened or worsened by this round's changes
- Realistische Bruchbedingung: Realistic break condition: a future change that allows two distinct work units to legitimately share one fingerprint (e.g., a batched multi-slice validation run) would let a review approved for one work unit satisfy the authorization check for a binding belonging to the other, since &#96;resolve_resume_state()&#96;'s new check never cross-validates &#96;ReviewPayload.work_unit_id&#96; against the binding's own owning work unit.
- Eigene Findings: `C-01`, `C-02`, `C-03`, `C-04`, `C-05`, `C-06`, `C-07`, `C-08`, `C-09`, `C-10`, `C-11`, `C-12`, `C-13`, `C-14`, `C-15`, `C-16`, `C-17`, `C-18`, `C-19`, `C-20`, `C-21`, `C-22`

- Größtes Restrisiko: Largest residual risk: In ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_structured_baseline, plan_payload derives approved_plan_commit from state.current_slice.start_commit instead of pinning to the immutable initial plan approval commit (e.g., state.slices[0].start_commit); in multi-slice workflows under structured-v1, this will create redundant approved-plan revisions on each slice transition attributing the approved plan commit to the slice start boundary.

### Ereignis 5: Runde 1

- Reviewer: `antigravity`
- Freigabe: `YES`
- Validierungsbindung: `validation-2e0d30731579`
- Testdateien: `tests/test_artifact_migration.py`, `tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py`
- Prüfdimensionen: (1) Correctness &amp; Referential Integrity: resolve_resume_state() in src/artifact_migration.py verifies that every BindingPayload references an existing ValidationAttestationPayload and approved ReviewPayload records with matching diff fingerprints, fail-closing against fabricated or mismatched provenance; (2) Security Boundaries &amp; Failure Paths: _recover_completed_reviewer_contract() strictly restricts contract recovery to AgentFailureKind.AUTH, preventing QUOTA errors from bypassing quota wait cycles and audit trail persistence; (3) Resume &amp; Idempotency: All resume cross-checks remain symmetric and fail-closed across task, plan, gate, quota, attestation, review, and completion facts; (4) Test Coverage: Parametrized negative tests in test_artifact_migration.py and integration tests in test_orchestrator_runtime.py cover unknown, mismatched, and unapproved binding references as well as QUOTA retry and QuotaPausePayload persistence
- Größtes Restrisiko: The referential integrity check in resolve_resume_state() validates diff fingerprint matching and approval verdict for BindingPayload.approval_ids, but does not cross-validate ReviewPayload.work_unit_id against the binding's owning work unit ID
- Realistische Bruchbedingung: A future orchestrator change that enables multiple distinct work units to batch or share identical diff fingerprints could permit an approved ReviewPayload from one work unit to satisfy the referential integrity check for a BindingPayload originating from another work unit sharing that same fingerprint
- Eigene Findings: keine
<!-- audit:antigravity-review:end -->

- `C-01` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Zero-Dependency-Vertrag wiederhergestellt; der exakte Akzeptanztest besteht.
- `C-02` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Wie vorgesehen auf Slice 02 und dessen Store-Invarianten vertagt.
- `C-02` Antwort 2: **angenommen** — Failed- und stopped-Completion-Records mit final_binding_id werden durch die Store-Invariante abgewiesen und durch Slice-02-Negativtests abgedeckt.
- `C-03` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Durable-but-reported-failed-Vertrag dokumentiert und persisted=True-Scanpfad direkt getestet.
- `C-04` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Work-Unit-Idempotenzschlüssel enthalten nun die Rundennummer; derselbe Round bleibt idempotent, Round-Wechsel erzeugen geordnete Revisionen. Der exakte Regressionstest und 25 fokussierte Tests bestehen.
- `C-05` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Symmetrische Gate- und Finding-Prüfungen erkennen nun Mirror-Fakten ohne Chain-Gegenstück; beide geforderten Regressionstests bestehen.
- `C-06` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Mirror-seitige Quota-Pausen ohne korrespondierenden Chain-Record lösen jetzt fail-closed einen ArtifactResumeError aus.
- `C-07` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — German runtime label replaced with “Approvals”; exact language-consistency guard passes.
- `C-08` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Finding transitions are intentionally run-wide because lifecycles may span original and correction Slices; documented and covered by a cross-Slice regression test.
- `C-09` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Gates, Validierungsanforderungen, Attestierungen und Bindungen anderer Slices werden ausgeschlossen; lokale operative Records bleiben enthalten.
- `C-10` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Eigene Gate- und Validierungsrecords sind über den Codex-AgentResult-Fingerprint bereits vor dem ersten Review sichtbar; fremde Slice-Records bleiben ausgeschlossen.
- `C-11` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Die geforderte Runtime-Regression betrifft Slice-06-Pfade außerhalb der aktuellen Allowlist und bleibt für einen autorisierten Korrekturslice offen.
- `C-11` Antwort 2: **angenommen** — Die fehlende Regression und Dokumentation für die protokollabhängige Behandlung von --test-command besteht fort; insbesondere sind quoted arguments, Shelloperator-Kompatibilität und Legacy-Parität nicht abgesichert.
- `C-12` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Die geforderte Resume-Regression beziehungsweise Code-Dokumentation betrifft Slice-06-Pfade außerhalb der aktuellen Allowlist und bleibt für einen autorisierten Korrekturslice offen.
- `C-12` Antwort 2: **angenommen** — Der reale Stop-Pfad zeigt zusätzlich einen konkreten Resume-Blocker: Das strukturierte Stop-Review wird persistiert, aber nicht in den ReviewAuditEvent-Mirror übernommen, sodass der nächste Decision-Context-Abgleich Recordkette und State-v3 als divergent zurückweist.
- `C-12` Antwort 3: **angenommen** — Stop-Reviews werden vor dem Halt in runtime_history gespiegelt; Stop/Resume-Regression und 75 fokussierte Tests bestehen.
- `C-13` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Die Dokumentationsprüfung bleibt von der realen ArtifactStore-Pfad- und Dateinamenskonstruktion entkoppelt und kann bei einer späteren Layoutänderung falsch positiv bestehen.

- `C-01` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Zero-Dependency-Vertrag wiederhergestellt; der exakte Akzeptanztest besteht.
- `C-02` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Wie vorgesehen auf Slice 02 und dessen Store-Invarianten vertagt.
- `C-02` Antwort 2: **angenommen** — Failed- und stopped-Completion-Records mit final_binding_id werden durch die Store-Invariante abgewiesen und durch Slice-02-Negativtests abgedeckt.
- `C-03` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Durable-but-reported-failed-Vertrag dokumentiert und persisted=True-Scanpfad direkt getestet.
- `C-04` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Work-Unit-Idempotenzschlüssel enthalten nun die Rundennummer; derselbe Round bleibt idempotent, Round-Wechsel erzeugen geordnete Revisionen. Der exakte Regressionstest und 25 fokussierte Tests bestehen.
- `C-05` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Symmetrische Gate- und Finding-Prüfungen erkennen nun Mirror-Fakten ohne Chain-Gegenstück; beide geforderten Regressionstests bestehen.
- `C-06` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Mirror-seitige Quota-Pausen ohne korrespondierenden Chain-Record lösen jetzt fail-closed einen ArtifactResumeError aus.
- `C-07` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — German runtime label replaced with “Approvals”; exact language-consistency guard passes.
- `C-08` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Finding transitions are intentionally run-wide because lifecycles may span original and correction Slices; documented and covered by a cross-Slice regression test.
- `C-09` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Gates, Validierungsanforderungen, Attestierungen und Bindungen anderer Slices werden ausgeschlossen; lokale operative Records bleiben enthalten.
- `C-10` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Eigene Gate- und Validierungsrecords sind über den Codex-AgentResult-Fingerprint bereits vor dem ersten Review sichtbar; fremde Slice-Records bleiben ausgeschlossen.
- `C-11` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Die geforderte Runtime-Regression betrifft Slice-06-Pfade außerhalb der aktuellen Allowlist und bleibt für einen autorisierten Korrekturslice offen.
- `C-11` Antwort 2: **angenommen** — Die fehlende Regression und Dokumentation für die protokollabhängige Behandlung von --test-command besteht fort; insbesondere sind quoted arguments, Shelloperator-Kompatibilität und Legacy-Parität nicht abgesichert.
- `C-12` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Die geforderte Resume-Regression beziehungsweise Code-Dokumentation betrifft Slice-06-Pfade außerhalb der aktuellen Allowlist und bleibt für einen autorisierten Korrekturslice offen.
- `C-12` Antwort 2: **angenommen** — Der reale Stop-Pfad zeigt zusätzlich einen konkreten Resume-Blocker: Das strukturierte Stop-Review wird persistiert, aber nicht in den ReviewAuditEvent-Mirror übernommen, sodass der nächste Decision-Context-Abgleich Recordkette und State-v3 als divergent zurückweist.
- `C-12` Antwort 3: **angenommen** — Stop-Reviews werden vor dem Halt in runtime_history gespiegelt; Stop/Resume-Regression und 75 fokussierte Tests bestehen.
- `C-13` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Die Dokumentationsprüfung bleibt von der realen ArtifactStore-Pfad- und Dateinamenskonstruktion entkoppelt und kann bei einer späteren Layoutänderung falsch positiv bestehen.
- `C-14` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Quota-Pausen werden idempotent als QuotaPausePayload persistiert; Resume-Abgleich und exakter Regressionstest bestehen.
- `C-15` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Korrektur-Work-Units persistieren offene Finding-IDs als CorrectionWorkUnitPayload; additive Kompatibilität mit vorhandenen WorkUnit-Records ist gewährleistet.
- `C-16` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — PlanPayload und Resume-Abgleich verwenden jetzt den ursprünglichen persistierten approved_plan_commit statt des wechselnden Slice-Start-Commits.
- `C-17` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Legacy-shaped structured-v1 states without approved_plan_commit can no longer bypass plan-chain validation; multiple PlanPayload records halt resume with ArtifactResumeError.
- `C-18` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Resume-Planbindung wird nur im IMPLEMENT-Modus geprüft; legitime frühe PLAN_ONLY-Zustände bleiben zulässig, während C-17 fail-closed bleibt. Alle 5 Akzeptanztests sowie 8 Artifact-Resume-Tests bestehen.
- `C-19` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Symmetrischer Completion-Abgleich implementiert und durch den geforderten Regressionstest abgesichert.
- `C-20` Antwort 1: **angenommen** — Completion-Abgleich entspricht nun Writer und Workflow-Terminalsemantik; legitime Post-Slice-Checkpoints bleiben zulässig, echte terminale Mirrors ohne WorkflowCompletionPayload bleiben fail-closed.

- Pre-Mortems:
  - Ereignis 4: In three months, the most likely failure is a later slice (02+) adding a new schema property/pattern without re-anchoring it or without extending &#96;_check_schema_node&#96;'s keyword allowlist test coverage, causing the hand-rolled Draft-2020-12 subset validator to silently accept malformed records that a real &#96;jsonschema&#96; library would reject — this is the direct cost of trading the removed dependency for a bespoke, narrower validator.
  - Ereignis 5: In three months, the most likely failure cause is that a later slice introduces complex cross-record validation or schema extensions that diverge subtly from the hand-rolled Draft-2020-12 subset validator in &#96;src/artifact_models.py&#96;, leading to edge cases where Python domain dataclass &#96;__post_init__&#96; checks and schema validation have mismatched strictness.

- Pre-Mortems:
  - Ereignis 2: In three months, the most likely failure is a Slice-03/04 caller misinterpreting a &#96;put()&#96; exception as "nothing was written" (retrying with a different idempotency key, or silently skipping the legacy-text mirror write), producing a divergent dual-write pair or a duplicate structured record — because the durable-but-possibly-erroring contract on &#96;put()&#96; is proven by test but not yet a documented API guarantee that later slices can rely on without re-deriving it from source.
  - Ereignis 3: In three months, the most likely failure cause is a future dual-write adapter or recovery routine in Slice 03/04 attempting to resume or retry an interrupted step under a freshly minted idempotency key or misaligned predecessor pointer, resulting in an unhandled ArtifactConflictError instead of leveraging the store's idempotent retry mechanism.

  - Ereignis 5: In three months, the most likely failure cause is a future slice during the structured cutover introducing a new payload field that mutates across workflow lifecycle transitions (such as dynamic remediation scope extensions or user gate overrides) while retaining a static or partially-keyed idempotency key, causing ArtifactBridge._assert_equal to trip an unhandled ArtifactBridgeError upon rebind/resume.

  - Ereignis 7: In three months, the most likely failure cause will be an uncoordinated evolution between state mirror structures and record payload schemas during Slice 5/6 cutover (such as adding an optional metadata field to TaskPayload or WorkUnitPayload without updating both resolve_resume_state() and ArtifactBridge._assert_equal()), causing valid dual-write runs to abruptly halt with ArtifactResumeError on resume when encountering states generated across slightly differing build versions.

### `C-03` — `OPEN`

### `C-04` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_structured_baseline()&#96; (invoked unconditionally, uncaught, from every &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call — i.e. every rebind/resume within a work unit) derives the &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96; idempotency key solely from &#96;unit.work_unit_id&#96; (&#96;idempotency_key=f"work-unit:{unit.work_unit_id}"&#96;), while the payload it constructs embeds &#96;round_number=unit.round_number&#96;. Elsewhere in the same diff (&#96;persist_codex_contract&#96;'s &#96;logical=f"agent-{unit.work_unit_id}-{state.current_step.value}-{unit.round_number}"&#96;, and &#96;persist_review_contract&#96;'s explicit &#96;round_number&#96; parameter used in its own idempotency key) the codebase treats &#96;round_number&#96; as a value that legitimately changes across multiple rounds within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96; (repair/retry/second-review cycles). The first &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call for round 1 durably publishes &#96;WorkUnitPayload(round_number=1,...)&#96; under key &#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;&#96;. As soon as the same work unit advances to round 2 and &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; fires again, &#96;ArtifactBridge.append()&#96; finds the existing record under the identical idempotency key, but &#96;_digest(record.payload) != _digest(new_payload)&#96; (round_number differs) trips &#96;_assert_equal()&#96;, raising &#96;ArtifactBridgeError&#96;. &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96; is called directly, not through &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96;'s try/except-to-&#96;WorkflowExecutionError&#96; wrapper, so this exception is not fail-closed gracefully — it propagates as a raw, undocumented crash out of &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96;, which is the very first thing the driver does on every resume. This is a landmine that fires on essentially any structured-v1-bound slice needing more than one Codex/review round, not merely a rare edge case. The only existing coverage (&#96;test_structured_bind_persists_contract_and_active_work_unit_once&#96;) calls &#96;bind_work_unit(state)&#96; twice with the SAME unchanged &#96;state&#96; object, so it cannot and does not exercise a round-number transition within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96;; the defect is unguarded by any test.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_structured_bind_survives_round_number_increase_within_same_work_unit","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Idempotency key for WorkUnitPayload now includes round_number (&#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;:round:&lt;n&gt;&#96;), so same-round re-binds stay idempotent and round transitions persist as a new ordered revision instead of raising ArtifactBridgeError. The exact acceptance test (test_structured_bind_survives_round_number_increase_within_same_work_unit) is present, exercises exactly the previously-broken transition, and passes in the bound 744-test attestation for the current fingerprint. Closing as originally reported by me.

The only existing coverage (&#96;test_structured_bind_persists_contract_and_active_work_unit_once&#96;) calls &#96;bind_work_unit(state)&#96; twice with the SAME unchanged &#96;state&#96; object, so it cannot and does not exercise a round-number transition within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96;; the defect is unguarded by any test.

### `C-02` — `CLOSED`

### `C-03` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `OBSERVATION`
- Finding: &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; can raise an exception (e.g., a transient error during the mandatory post-publish re-scan/head-cache refresh) after the record bytes are already durably and atomically published to &#96;records_dir&#96;, and this "durable-but-reported-as-failed" retry contract is exercised by one crash-recovery test but is not documented as an explicit API guarantee on &#96;put()&#96;. Slice 03 (dual-write/semantic equality) will depend on exactly this guarantee to decide whether to retry vs. treat an exception as "nothing was written," so an undocumented/underspecified contract here risks a double-write or a lost-write assumption once callers are wired in.
- Akzeptanztest: Add a docstring/contract note on &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; stating that any exception raised after bytes are written to &#96;records_dir&#96; must be treated as "possibly already persisted, safe to retry the identical call," and add a scan-time test that directly (via a hand-written envelope, bypassing &#96;put()&#96;) exercises the &#96;persisted=True&#96; branch of &#96;_validate_store_invariants&#96; so the &#96;load_chain()&#96;-side completion-invariant rejection path has direct coverage independent of &#96;put()&#96;.
- Statusbegründung: Acceptance criteria fully met in this diff: &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; now carries an explicit docstring contract ("An exception does not prove that nothing was written... callers must treat every exception as possibly already persisted and may safely retry the identical logical call"), and &#96;tests/test_artifact_store.py::test_scan_rejects_persisted_non_success_completion_with_binding&#96; directly exercises the &#96;persisted=True&#96; scan branch via a hand-written envelope that bypasses &#96;put()&#96;, matching my Slice-02 acceptance test verbatim. Closing as originally reported by me.

### `C-04` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_structured_baseline()&#96; (invoked unconditionally, uncaught, from every &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call — i.e. every rebind/resume within a work unit) derives the &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96; idempotency key solely from &#96;unit.work_unit_id&#96; (&#96;idempotency_key=f"work-unit:{unit.work_unit_id}"&#96;), while the payload it constructs embeds &#96;round_number=unit.round_number&#96;. Elsewhere in the same diff (&#96;persist_codex_contract&#96;'s &#96;logical=f"agent-{unit.work_unit_id}-{state.current_step.value}-{unit.round_number}"&#96;, and &#96;persist_review_contract&#96;'s explicit &#96;round_number&#96; parameter used in its own idempotency key) the codebase treats &#96;round_number&#96; as a value that legitimately changes across multiple rounds within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96; (repair/retry/second-review cycles). The first &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call for round 1 durably publishes &#96;WorkUnitPayload(round_number=1,...)&#96; under key &#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;&#96;. As soon as the same work unit advances to round 2 and &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; fires again, &#96;ArtifactBridge.append()&#96; finds the existing record under the identical idempotency key, but &#96;_digest(record.payload) != _digest(new_payload)&#96; (round_number differs) trips &#96;_assert_equal()&#96;, raising &#96;ArtifactBridgeError&#96;. &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96; is called directly, not through &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96;'s try/except-to-&#96;WorkflowExecutionError&#96; wrapper, so this exception is not fail-closed gracefully — it propagates as a raw, undocumented crash out of &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96;, which is the very first thing the driver does on every resume. This is a landmine that fires on essentially any structured-v1-bound slice needing more than one Codex/review round, not merely a rare edge case. The only existing coverage (&#96;test_structured_bind_persists_contract_and_active_work_unit_once&#96;) calls &#96;bind_work_unit(state)&#96; twice with the SAME unchanged &#96;state&#96; object, so it cannot and does not exercise a round-number transition within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96;; the defect is unguarded by any test.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_structured_bind_survives_round_number_increase_within_same_work_unit","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Idempotency key for WorkUnitPayload now includes round_number (&#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;:round:&lt;n&gt;&#96;), so same-round re-binds stay idempotent and round transitions persist as a new ordered revision instead of raising ArtifactBridgeError. The exact acceptance test (test_structured_bind_survives_round_number_increase_within_same_work_unit) is present, exercises exactly the previously-broken transition, and passes in the bound 744-test attestation for the current fingerprint. Closing as originally reported by me.

### `C-05` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;resolve_resume_state()&#96; in &#96;src/artifact_migration.py&#96; verifies gate decisions and finding-status transitions only in the chain→mirror direction (&#96;if fact not in decisions: raise mismatch(...)&#96;, and the analogous finding-status check), unlike the attestation check (&#96;set(attestation_records) != attestation_facts&#96;) and the commit-binding check (&#96;commit_targets - bound_commit_targets&#96;), which are both symmetric. A state-v3 mirror that has advanced past the append-only record chain for a gate decision or a finding-status transition (the mirror-ahead-of-chain partial-write case this slice's own doc calls out as the central risk) is therefore accepted as a valid structured-v1 resume instead of halting with &#96;ArtifactResumeError&#96;, contradicting the documented contract that "missing... spiegelbare Fakten" must produce a resumable repair halt. No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_migration.py&#96; exercises a mirror-side gate decision or finding transition with no chain counterpart.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_artifact_migration.py::test_structured_resume_halts_when_mirror_gate_decision_has_no_chain_record","tests/test_artifact_migration.py::test_structured_resume_halts_when_mirror_finding_transition_has_no_chain_record","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Exact acceptance test (two named regression tests) present and passing in the bound attestation; fix applies the same symmetric-set pattern already used for attestations/commit-bindings to gate decisions and finding transitions, correctly closing the mirror-ahead-of-chain gap I reported.

### `C-09` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 2
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;ArtifactAuditProjection.selected_records&#96; in &#96;src/artifact_projection.py&#96; scopes only &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;CorrectionWorkUnitPayload&#96; directly by &#96;slice_id&#96;, and any other payload type only via an ad-hoc &#96;getattr(payload, "work_unit_id", None)&#96; correlation to that Slice's own work-unit IDs (used for e.g. &#96;ReviewPayload&#96;). &#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;, and &#96;BindingPayload&#96; — rendered into the &#96;test-approval-premortem&#96;, &#96;validation-attestation&#96;, and &#96;approval-status&#96; sections — carry no &#96;work_unit_id&#96; field at all (none of their render branches in the same file reference &#96;payload.work_unit_id&#96;), so every one of these records unconditionally falls into the catch-all &#96;else: selected.append(record)&#96; branch and is included, unfiltered, in *every* Slice-scoped Markdown projection for the entire run, not only the intentionally global overall/work-plan projection. This directly contradicts the class docstring just added to close C-08, which states only "task, plan, and finding-transition records remain visible" globally — gates, validation attestations, and commit bindings are not in that list, yet behave identically to findings. &#96;src/orchestrator.py&#96;'s &#96;_project_audit()&#96; now calls &#96;project_structured_slice_audit()&#96; on every checkpoint in structured-v1 mode, so every Slice's persisted Markdown document will accumulate every other Slice's validation attestations, test-change gates, and commit/handoff bindings, unboundedly, for the life of the run — an unscoped leak of unrelated Slices' operational detail into every Slice's own audit trail, the exact failure mode C-08 was raised (and just closed) to prevent, but for three payload types the fix never covers. No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_projection.py&#96; exercises Slice-exclusion for &#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, or &#96;BindingPayload&#96;; the only new regression test (&#96;test_slice_projection_accepts_chain_subsequence_and_excludes_other_work_unit&#96;) proves scoping solely for &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;ReviewPayload&#96; and intentional global scope solely for &#96;FindingTransitionPayload&#96;.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_artifact_projection.py::test_slice_projection_excludes_other_slice_gate_validation_and_binding_records","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: The reported cross-slice leak for GatePayload/ValidationRequestPayload/ValidationAttestationPayload/BindingPayload is fixed: &#96;selected_records&#96; now builds &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96; from records whose payload's &#96;work_unit_id&#96; belongs to this slice's own work units, and includes any ownerless-payload record whose &#96;record.fingerprint&#96; is in that set. Hand-traced the new test: slice-6 records (work-unit-13, review-13, validation-request, validation-attestation, gate, binding — all fingerprint &#96;c*64&#96;) are excluded from the &#96;slice_id="5"&#96; projection, while &#96;_chain()&#96;'s own gate/validation/binding (fingerprint &#96;a*64&#96;, linked via that round's &#96;ReviewPayload(work_unit_id="12")&#96;) remain visible. Exact acceptance test I set is present and passes in the bound 765-test attestation. Closing as originally reported by me.

### `C-10` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 3
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: The C-09 fix correlates ownerless payload types (&#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;, &#96;BindingPayload&#96;) to a Slice exclusively via &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96;, and &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96; is populated only from records whose payload exposes a &#96;work_unit_id&#96; attribute — across every payload type in &#96;artifact_models&#96;/&#96;artifact_projection&#96;, that is &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; alone (&#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;CorrectionWorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;GatePayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;/&#96;BindingPayload&#96;/&#96;TaskPayload&#96;/&#96;PlanPayload&#96;/&#96;FindingTransitionPayload&#96; never populate it). Consequently, any Gate/ValidationRequest/ValidationAttestation/Binding record produced for a Slice's own active round is invisible in that Slice's own &#96;render_sections()&#96; / persisted Markdown document until a &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; carrying the identical &#96;record.fingerprint&#96; is later appended for that exact round. &#96;src/orchestrator.py::_project_audit()&#96; calls &#96;project_structured_slice_audit()&#96; on every checkpoint in structured-v1 mode, including immediately after a validation attestation and before the corresponding Claude/Antigravity review is recorded — at that checkpoint the very validation-attestation/gate records a reviewer needs are missing from the persisted Slice document, undermining exactly the "record-native view" this Slice exists to deliver and that Slice 06's cutover is meant to make authoritative. Worse: if a round's implementation is superseded (its validation fails and the work unit advances to a new round/fingerprint without ever producing a &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; for the failed round's exact fingerprint — a normal repair/retry path this same diff series already treats as legitimate, e.g. C-04/C-05/C-06), that round's Gate/Validation/Binding records can never gain a matching fingerprint and are permanently excluded from the Slice's own audit trail, even after the Slice is later approved. No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_projection.py&#96; exercises a Gate/ValidationAttestation/Binding record for the Slice's own round before a matching-fingerprint &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; exists; the only new checkpoint-level test (&#96;test_structured_checkpoint_projects_record_chain_into_slice_and_overall_audits&#96;) only asserts a generic digest heading and a scope path, not this section's content, so the gap is unguarded.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_artifact_projection.py::test_slice_projection_includes_own_round_gate_and_validation_records_before_first_review","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Structural fix anchors slice_fingerprints on AgentResultPayload/DiagnosticPayload/ReviewPayload (not ReviewPayload alone), making a round's own Gate/ValidationRequest/ValidationAttestation records visible before the first Review via the Codex AgentResultPayload's fingerprint, and keeping superseded rounds' records reachable without requiring a matching-fingerprint Review. Exact acceptance test present and passing in the bound full-suite attestation (766 passed) for fingerprint 615044a6b8d702f824be3d0d81c2213e6d8a49b933ff962885580dc2a86e10f5; existing C-09 cross-Slice exclusion tests remain green, confirming no regression. Closing as originally reported by me.

- Akzeptanztest: Add a docstring/contract note on &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; stating that any exception raised after bytes are written to &#96;records_dir&#96; must be treated as "possibly already persisted, safe to retry the identical call," and add a scan-time test that directly (via a hand-written envelope, bypassing &#96;put()&#96;) exercises the &#96;persisted=True&#96; branch of &#96;_validate_store_invariants&#96; so the &#96;load_chain()&#96;-side completion-invariant rejection path has direct coverage independent of &#96;put()&#96;.
- Statusbegründung: Acceptance criteria fully met in this diff: &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; now carries an explicit docstring contract ("An exception does not prove that nothing was written... callers must treat every exception as possibly already persisted and may safely retry the identical logical call"), and &#96;tests/test_artifact_store.py::test_scan_rejects_persisted_non_success_completion_with_binding&#96; directly exercises the &#96;persisted=True&#96; scan branch via a hand-written envelope that bypasses &#96;put()&#96;, matching my Slice-02 acceptance test verbatim. Closing as originally reported by me.

### `C-04` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_structured_baseline()&#96; (invoked unconditionally, uncaught, from every &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call — i.e. every rebind/resume within a work unit) derives the &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96; idempotency key solely from &#96;unit.work_unit_id&#96; (&#96;idempotency_key=f"work-unit:{unit.work_unit_id}"&#96;), while the payload it constructs embeds &#96;round_number=unit.round_number&#96;. Elsewhere in the same diff (&#96;persist_codex_contract&#96;'s &#96;logical=f"agent-{unit.work_unit_id}-{state.current_step.value}-{unit.round_number}"&#96;, and &#96;persist_review_contract&#96;'s explicit &#96;round_number&#96; parameter used in its own idempotency key) the codebase treats &#96;round_number&#96; as a value that legitimately changes across multiple rounds within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96; (repair/retry/second-review cycles). The first &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call for round 1 durably publishes &#96;WorkUnitPayload(round_number=1,...)&#96; under key &#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;&#96;. As soon as the same work unit advances to round 2 and &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; fires again, &#96;ArtifactBridge.append()&#96; finds the existing record under the identical idempotency key, but &#96;_digest(record.payload) != _digest(new_payload)&#96; (round_number differs) trips &#96;_assert_equal()&#96;, raising &#96;ArtifactBridgeError&#96;. &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96; is called directly, not through &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96;'s try/except-to-&#96;WorkflowExecutionError&#96; wrapper, so this exception is not fail-closed gracefully — it propagates as a raw, undocumented crash out of &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96;, which is the very first thing the driver does on every resume. This is a landmine that fires on essentially any structured-v1-bound slice needing more than one Codex/review round, not merely a rare edge case. The only existing coverage (&#96;test_structured_bind_persists_contract_and_active_work_unit_once&#96;) calls &#96;bind_work_unit(state)&#96; twice with the SAME unchanged &#96;state&#96; object, so it cannot and does not exercise a round-number transition within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96;; the defect is unguarded by any test.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_structured_bind_survives_round_number_increase_within_same_work_unit","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Idempotency key for WorkUnitPayload now includes round_number (&#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;:round:&lt;n&gt;&#96;), so same-round re-binds stay idempotent and round transitions persist as a new ordered revision instead of raising ArtifactBridgeError. The exact acceptance test (test_structured_bind_survives_round_number_increase_within_same_work_unit) is present, exercises exactly the previously-broken transition, and passes in the bound 744-test attestation for the current fingerprint. Closing as originally reported by me.

### `C-05` — `CLOSED`

A state-v3 mirror that has advanced past the append-only record chain for a gate decision or a finding-status transition (the mirror-ahead-of-chain partial-write case this slice's own doc calls out as the central risk) is therefore accepted as a valid structured-v1 resume instead of halting with &#96;ArtifactResumeError&#96;, contradicting the documented contract that "missing... spiegelbare Fakten" must produce a resumable repair halt. No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_migration.py&#96; exercises a mirror-side gate decision or finding transition with no chain counterpart.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_artifact_migration.py::test_structured_resume_halts_when_mirror_gate_decision_has_no_chain_record","tests/test_artifact_migration.py::test_structured_resume_halts_when_mirror_finding_transition_has_no_chain_record","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Exact acceptance test (two named regression tests) present and passing in the bound attestation; fix applies the same symmetric-set pattern already used for attestations/commit-bindings to gate decisions and finding transitions, correctly closing the mirror-ahead-of-chain gap I reported.

### `C-09` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 2
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;ArtifactAuditProjection.selected_records&#96; in &#96;src/artifact_projection.py&#96; scopes only &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;CorrectionWorkUnitPayload&#96; directly by &#96;slice_id&#96;, and any other payload type only via an ad-hoc &#96;getattr(payload, "work_unit_id", None)&#96; correlation to that Slice's own work-unit IDs (used for e.g. &#96;ReviewPayload&#96;). &#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;, and &#96;BindingPayload&#96; — rendered into the &#96;test-approval-premortem&#96;, &#96;validation-attestation&#96;, and &#96;approval-status&#96; sections — carry no &#96;work_unit_id&#96; field at all (none of their render branches in the same file reference &#96;payload.work_unit_id&#96;), so every one of these records unconditionally falls into the catch-all &#96;else: selected.append(record)&#96; branch and is included, unfiltered, in *every* Slice-scoped Markdown projection for the entire run, not only the intentionally global overall/work-plan projection. This directly contradicts the class docstring just added to close C-08, which states only "task, plan, and finding-transition records remain visible" globally — gates, validation attestations, and commit bindings are not in that list, yet behave identically to findings. &#96;src/orchestrator.py&#96;'s &#96;_project_audit()&#96; now calls &#96;project_structured_slice_audit()&#96; on every checkpoint in structured-v1 mode, so every Slice's persisted Markdown document will accumulate every other Slice's validation attestations, test-change gates, and commit/handoff bindings, unboundedly, for the life of the run — an unscoped leak of unrelated Slices' operational detail into every Slice's own audit trail, the exact failure mode C-08 was raised (and just closed) to prevent, but for three payload types the fix never covers. No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_projection.py&#96; exercises Slice-exclusion for &#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, or &#96;BindingPayload&#96;; the only new regression test (&#96;test_slice_projection_accepts_chain_subsequence_and_excludes_other_work_unit&#96;) proves scoping solely for &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;ReviewPayload&#96; and intentional global scope solely for &#96;FindingTransitionPayload&#96;.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_artifact_projection.py::test_slice_projection_excludes_other_slice_gate_validation_and_binding_records","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: The reported cross-slice leak for GatePayload/ValidationRequestPayload/ValidationAttestationPayload/BindingPayload is fixed: &#96;selected_records&#96; now builds &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96; from records whose payload's &#96;work_unit_id&#96; belongs to this slice's own work units, and includes any ownerless-payload record whose &#96;record.fingerprint&#96; is in that set. Hand-traced the new test: slice-6 records (work-unit-13, review-13, validation-request, validation-attestation, gate, binding — all fingerprint &#96;c*64&#96;) are excluded from the &#96;slice_id="5"&#96; projection, while &#96;_chain()&#96;'s own gate/validation/binding (fingerprint &#96;a*64&#96;, linked via that round's &#96;ReviewPayload(work_unit_id="12")&#96;) remain visible. Exact acceptance test I set is present and passes in the bound 765-test attestation. Closing as originally reported by me.

### `C-10` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 3
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: The C-09 fix correlates ownerless payload types (&#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;, &#96;BindingPayload&#96;) to a Slice exclusively via &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96;, and &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96; is populated only from records whose payload exposes a &#96;work_unit_id&#96; attribute — across every payload type in &#96;artifact_models&#96;/&#96;artifact_projection&#96;, that is &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; alone (&#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;CorrectionWorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;GatePayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;/&#96;BindingPayload&#96;/&#96;TaskPayload&#96;/&#96;PlanPayload&#96;/&#96;FindingTransitionPayload&#96; never populate it). Consequently, any Gate/ValidationRequest/ValidationAttestation/Binding record produced for a Slice's own active round is invisible in that Slice's own &#96;render_sections()&#96; / persisted Markdown document until a &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; carrying the identical &#96;record.fingerprint&#96; is later appended for that exact round. &#96;src/orchestrator.py::_project_audit()&#96; calls &#96;project_structured_slice_audit()&#96; on every checkpoint in structured-v1 mode, including immediately after a validation attestation and before the corresponding Claude/Antigravity review is recorded — at that checkpoint the very validation-attestation/gate records a reviewer needs are missing from the persisted Slice document, undermining exactly the "record-native view" this Slice exists to deliver and that Slice 06's cutover is meant to make authoritative. Worse: if a round's implementation is superseded (its validation fails and the work unit advances to a new round/fingerprint without ever producing a &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; for the failed round's exact fingerprint — a normal repair/retry path this same diff series already treats as legitimate, e.g. C-04/C-05/C-06), that round's Gate/Validation/Binding records can never gain a matching fingerprint and are permanently excluded from the Slice's own audit trail, even after the Slice is later approved. No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_projection.py&#96; exercises a Gate/ValidationAttestation/Binding record for the Slice's own round before a matching-fingerprint &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; exists; the only new checkpoint-level test (&#96;test_structured_checkpoint_projects_record_chain_into_slice_and_overall_audits&#96;) only asserts a generic digest heading and a scope path, not this section's content, so the gap is unguarded.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_artifact_projection.py::test_slice_projection_includes_own_round_gate_and_validation_records_before_first_review","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Structural fix anchors slice_fingerprints on AgentResultPayload/DiagnosticPayload/ReviewPayload (not ReviewPayload alone), making a round's own Gate/ValidationRequest/ValidationAttestation records visible before the first Review via the Codex AgentResultPayload's fingerprint, and keeping superseded rounds' records reachable without requiring a matching-fingerprint Review. Exact acceptance test present and passing in the bound full-suite attestation (766 passed) for fingerprint 615044a6b8d702f824be3d0d81c2213e6d8a49b933ff962885580dc2a86e10f5; existing C-09 cross-Slice exclusion tests remain green, confirming no regression. Closing as originally reported by me.

### `C-03` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `OBSERVATION`
- Finding: &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; can raise an exception (e.g., a transient error during the mandatory post-publish re-scan/head-cache refresh) after the record bytes are already durably and atomically published to &#96;records_dir&#96;, and this "durable-but-reported-as-failed" retry contract is exercised by one crash-recovery test but is not documented as an explicit API guarantee on &#96;put()&#96;. Slice 03 (dual-write/semantic equality) will depend on exactly this guarantee to decide whether to retry vs. treat an exception as "nothing was written," so an undocumented/underspecified contract here risks a double-write or a lost-write assumption once callers are wired in.
- Akzeptanztest: Add a docstring/contract note on &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; stating that any exception raised after bytes are written to &#96;records_dir&#96; must be treated as "possibly already persisted, safe to retry the identical call," and add a scan-time test that directly (via a hand-written envelope, bypassing &#96;put()&#96;) exercises the &#96;persisted=True&#96; branch of &#96;_validate_store_invariants&#96; so the &#96;load_chain()&#96;-side completion-invariant rejection path has direct coverage independent of &#96;put()&#96;.
- Statusbegründung: Acceptance criteria fully met in this diff: &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; now carries an explicit docstring contract ("An exception does not prove that nothing was written... callers must treat every exception as possibly already persisted and may safely retry the identical logical call"), and &#96;tests/test_artifact_store.py::test_scan_rejects_persisted_non_success_completion_with_binding&#96; directly exercises the &#96;persisted=True&#96; scan branch via a hand-written envelope that bypasses &#96;put()&#96;, matching my Slice-02 acceptance test verbatim. Closing as originally reported by me.

### `C-04` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_structured_baseline()&#96; (invoked unconditionally, uncaught, from every &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call — i.e. every rebind/resume within a work unit) derives the &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96; idempotency key solely from &#96;unit.work_unit_id&#96; (&#96;idempotency_key=f"work-unit:{unit.work_unit_id}"&#96;), while the payload it constructs embeds &#96;round_number=unit.round_number&#96;. Elsewhere in the same diff (&#96;persist_codex_contract&#96;'s &#96;logical=f"agent-{unit.work_unit_id}-{state.current_step.value}-{unit.round_number}"&#96;, and &#96;persist_review_contract&#96;'s explicit &#96;round_number&#96; parameter used in its own idempotency key) the codebase treats &#96;round_number&#96; as a value that legitimately changes across multiple rounds within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96; (repair/retry/second-review cycles). The first &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call for round 1 durably publishes &#96;WorkUnitPayload(round_number=1,...)&#96; under key &#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;&#96;. As soon as the same work unit advances to round 2 and &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; fires again, &#96;ArtifactBridge.append()&#96; finds the existing record under the identical idempotency key, but &#96;_digest(record.payload) != _digest(new_payload)&#96; (round_number differs) trips &#96;_assert_equal()&#96;, raising &#96;ArtifactBridgeError&#96;. &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96; is called directly, not through &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96;'s try/except-to-&#96;WorkflowExecutionError&#96; wrapper, so this exception is not fail-closed gracefully — it propagates as a raw, undocumented crash out of &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96;, which is the very first thing the driver does on every resume. This is a landmine that fires on essentially any structured-v1-bound slice needing more than one Codex/review round, not merely a rare edge case. The only existing coverage (&#96;test_structured_bind_persists_contract_and_active_work_unit_once&#96;) calls &#96;bind_work_unit(state)&#96; twice with the SAME unchanged &#96;state&#96; object, so it cannot and does not exercise a round-number transition within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96;; the defect is unguarded by any test.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_structured_bind_survives_round_number_increase_within_same_work_unit","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Idempotency key for WorkUnitPayload now includes round_number (&#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;:round:&lt;n&gt;&#96;), so same-round re-binds stay idempotent and round transitions persist as a new ordered revision instead of raising ArtifactBridgeError. The exact acceptance test (test_structured_bind_survives_round_number_increase_within_same_work_unit) is present, exercises exactly the previously-broken transition, and passes in the bound 744-test attestation for the current fingerprint. Closing as originally reported by me.

- Statusbegründung: Exact acceptance test (two named regression tests) present and passing in the bound attestation; fix applies the same symmetric-set pattern already used for attestations/commit-bindings to gate decisions and finding transitions, correctly closing the mirror-ahead-of-chain gap I reported.

No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_projection.py&#96; exercises Slice-exclusion for &#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, or &#96;BindingPayload&#96;; the only new regression test (&#96;test_slice_projection_accepts_chain_subsequence_and_excludes_other_work_unit&#96;) proves scoping solely for &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;ReviewPayload&#96; and intentional global scope solely for &#96;FindingTransitionPayload&#96;.

### `C-03` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `OBSERVATION`
- Finding: &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; can raise an exception (e.g., a transient error during the mandatory post-publish re-scan/head-cache refresh) after the record bytes are already durably and atomically published to &#96;records_dir&#96;, and this "durable-but-reported-as-failed" retry contract is exercised by one crash-recovery test but is not documented as an explicit API guarantee on &#96;put()&#96;. Slice 03 (dual-write/semantic equality) will depend on exactly this guarantee to decide whether to retry vs. treat an exception as "nothing was written," so an undocumented/underspecified contract here risks a double-write or a lost-write assumption once callers are wired in.
- Akzeptanztest: Add a docstring/contract note on &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; stating that any exception raised after bytes are written to &#96;records_dir&#96; must be treated as "possibly already persisted, safe to retry the identical call," and add a scan-time test that directly (via a hand-written envelope, bypassing &#96;put()&#96;) exercises the &#96;persisted=True&#96; branch of &#96;_validate_store_invariants&#96; so the &#96;load_chain()&#96;-side completion-invariant rejection path has direct coverage independent of &#96;put()&#96;.
- Statusbegründung: Verified fixed and re-checked against current source in this final review round; covered by bound attestation validation-ca6854031cff (775 passed/0 failed).

### `C-04` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_structured_baseline()&#96; (invoked unconditionally, uncaught, from every &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call — i.e. every rebind/resume within a work unit) derives the &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96; idempotency key solely from &#96;unit.work_unit_id&#96; (&#96;idempotency_key=f"work-unit:{unit.work_unit_id}"&#96;), while the payload it constructs embeds &#96;round_number=unit.round_number&#96;. Elsewhere in the same diff (&#96;persist_codex_contract&#96;'s &#96;logical=f"agent-{unit.work_unit_id}-{state.current_step.value}-{unit.round_number}"&#96;, and &#96;persist_review_contract&#96;'s explicit &#96;round_number&#96; parameter used in its own idempotency key) the codebase treats &#96;round_number&#96; as a value that legitimately changes across multiple rounds within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96; (repair/retry/second-review cycles). The first &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call for round 1 durably publishes &#96;WorkUnitPayload(round_number=1,...)&#96; under key &#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;&#96;. As soon as the same work unit advances to round 2 and &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; fires again, &#96;ArtifactBridge.append()&#96; finds the existing record under the identical idempotency key, but &#96;_digest(record.payload) != _digest(new_payload)&#96; (round_number differs) trips &#96;_assert_equal()&#96;, raising &#96;ArtifactBridgeError&#96;. &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96; is called directly, not through &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96;'s try/except-to-&#96;WorkflowExecutionError&#96; wrapper, so this exception is not fail-closed gracefully — it propagates as a raw, undocumented crash out of &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96;, which is the very first thing the driver does on every resume. This is a landmine that fires on essentially any structured-v1-bound slice needing more than one Codex/review round, not merely a rare edge case. The only existing coverage (&#96;test_structured_bind_persists_contract_and_active_work_unit_once&#96;) calls &#96;bind_work_unit(state)&#96; twice with the SAME unchanged &#96;state&#96; object, so it cannot and does not exercise a round-number transition within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96;; the defect is unguarded by any test.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_structured_bind_survives_round_number_increase_within_same_work_unit","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Verified fixed and re-checked against current source in this final review round; covered by bound attestation validation-ca6854031cff (775 passed/0 failed).

No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_migration.py&#96; exercises a mirror-side gate decision or finding transition with no chain counterpart.

### `C-09` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 2
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;ArtifactAuditProjection.selected_records&#96; in &#96;src/artifact_projection.py&#96; scopes only &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;CorrectionWorkUnitPayload&#96; directly by &#96;slice_id&#96;, and any other payload type only via an ad-hoc &#96;getattr(payload, "work_unit_id", None)&#96; correlation to that Slice's own work-unit IDs (used for e.g. &#96;ReviewPayload&#96;). &#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;, and &#96;BindingPayload&#96; — rendered into the &#96;test-approval-premortem&#96;, &#96;validation-attestation&#96;, and &#96;approval-status&#96; sections — carry no &#96;work_unit_id&#96; field at all (none of their render branches in the same file reference &#96;payload.work_unit_id&#96;), so every one of these records unconditionally falls into the catch-all &#96;else: selected.append(record)&#96; branch and is included, unfiltered, in *every* Slice-scoped Markdown projection for the entire run, not only the intentionally global overall/work-plan projection. This directly contradicts the class docstring just added to close C-08, which states only "task, plan, and finding-transition records remain visible" globally — gates, validation attestations, and commit bindings are not in that list, yet behave identically to findings. &#96;src/orchestrator.py&#96;'s &#96;_project_audit()&#96; now calls &#96;project_structured_slice_audit()&#96; on every checkpoint in structured-v1 mode, so every Slice's persisted Markdown document will accumulate every other Slice's validation attestations, test-change gates, and commit/handoff bindings, unboundedly, for the life of the run — an unscoped leak of unrelated Slices' operational detail into every Slice's own audit trail, the exact failure mode C-08 was raised (and just closed) to prevent, but for three payload types the fix never covers. No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_projection.py&#96; exercises Slice-exclusion for &#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, or &#96;BindingPayload&#96;; the only new regression test (&#96;test_slice_projection_accepts_chain_subsequence_and_excludes_other_work_unit&#96;) proves scoping solely for &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;ReviewPayload&#96; and intentional global scope solely for &#96;FindingTransitionPayload&#96;.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_artifact_projection.py::test_slice_projection_excludes_other_slice_gate_validation_and_binding_records","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Cross-Slice projection scoping fix confirmed in artifact_projection.py; re-verified against current source and bound attestation validation-ca6854031cff (775 passed/0 failed).

### `C-10` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 3
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: The C-09 fix correlates ownerless payload types (&#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;, &#96;BindingPayload&#96;) to a Slice exclusively via &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96;, and &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96; is populated only from records whose payload exposes a &#96;work_unit_id&#96; attribute — across every payload type in &#96;artifact_models&#96;/&#96;artifact_projection&#96;, that is &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; alone (&#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;CorrectionWorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;GatePayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;/&#96;BindingPayload&#96;/&#96;TaskPayload&#96;/&#96;PlanPayload&#96;/&#96;FindingTransitionPayload&#96; never populate it). Consequently, any Gate/ValidationRequest/ValidationAttestation/Binding record produced for a Slice's own active round is invisible in that Slice's own &#96;render_sections()&#96; / persisted Markdown document until a &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; carrying the identical &#96;record.fingerprint&#96; is later appended for that exact round. &#96;src/orchestrator.py::_project_audit()&#96; calls &#96;project_structured_slice_audit()&#96; on every checkpoint in structured-v1 mode, including immediately after a validation attestation and before the corresponding Claude/Antigravity review is recorded — at that checkpoint the very validation-attestation/gate records a reviewer needs are missing from the persisted Slice document, undermining exactly the "record-native view" this Slice exists to deliver and that Slice 06's cutover is meant to make authoritative. Worse: if a round's implementation is superseded (its validation fails and the work unit advances to a new round/fingerprint without ever producing a &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; for the failed round's exact fingerprint — a normal repair/retry path this same diff series already treats as legitimate, e.g. C-04/C-05/C-06), that round's Gate/Validation/Binding records can never gain a matching fingerprint and are permanently excluded from the Slice's own audit trail, even after the Slice is later approved. No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_projection.py&#96; exercises a Gate/ValidationAttestation/Binding record for the Slice's own round before a matching-fingerprint &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; exists; the only new checkpoint-level test (&#96;test_structured_checkpoint_projects_record_chain_into_slice_and_overall_audits&#96;) only asserts a generic digest heading and a scope path, not this section's content, so the gap is unguarded.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_artifact_projection.py::test_slice_projection_includes_own_round_gate_and_validation_records_before_first_review","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Cross-Slice projection scoping fix confirmed in artifact_projection.py; re-verified against current source and bound attestation validation-ca6854031cff (775 passed/0 failed).

### `C-14` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1

Since &#96;_fresh_state()&#96; now unconditionally binds every new run to &#96;structured-v1&#96; (Slice 06 cutover), any real quota/rate-limit pause-and-automatic-resume on a production run will permanently and unrecoverably break both process-restart resume and the in-process &#96;assert_structured_decision_context()&#96; guard invoked before every subsequent &#96;_agent&#96;/&#96;validate&#96;/&#96;commit_slice&#96;/&#96;finalize_audit&#96; call, with no code path able to self-heal the missing record. This is a severe, source-confirmed availability regression on the new mandatory default protocol.

- Finding: &#96;CorrectionWorkUnitPayload&#96; (schema + model, with required &#96;finding_ids&#96;) and its dedicated projection rendering ("Korrektur-Work-Unit" branch in artifact_projection.py) are unreachable dead code in production. &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_structured_baseline()&#96; unconditionally persists every non-PLAN work unit — including correction rounds — as a plain &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;, never branching on the correction &#96;WorkUnitKind&#96; to build &#96;CorrectionWorkUnitPayload&#96; with its finding attribution. Slice 05's own report explicitly flagged this exact gap as an accepted open risk to be "in den nachfolgenden Slices weiter gehärtet" (hardened in the following Slices, i.e. Slice 06's cutover), but Slice 06/08 never implemented it. Correction-round audit trails therefore permanently lose their finding-attribution record and render as ordinary Slice work units, defeating a stated Slice-01/05 design deliverable and the "declared requirements and acceptance criteria" / "dead transition states" dimensions explicitly called out for this final review. Does not break fail-closed resume (WORK_UNIT-type matching in resolve_resume_state still succeeds on paths/slice/round alone), but is a confirmed, traceable, unfulfilled architecture commitment.

Exact acceptance test &#96;tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_correction_work_unit_persists_correction_work_unit_payload_with_finding_ids&#96; is present and passes in the bound attestation.
