### `C-03` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `OBSERVATION`
- Finding: &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; can raise an exception (e.g., a transient error during the mandatory post-publish re-scan/head-cache refresh) after the record bytes are already durably and atomically published to &#96;records_dir&#96;, and this "durable-but-reported-as-failed" retry contract is exercised by one crash-recovery test but is not documented as an explicit API guarantee on &#96;put()&#96;. Slice 03 (dual-write/semantic equality) will depend on exactly this guarantee to decide whether to retry vs. treat an exception as "nothing was written," so an undocumented/underspecified contract here risks a double-write or a lost-write assumption once callers are wired in.
- Akzeptanztest: Add a docstring/contract note on &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; stating that any exception raised after bytes are written to &#96;records_dir&#96; must be treated as "possibly already persisted, safe to retry the identical call," and add a scan-time test that directly (via a hand-written envelope, bypassing &#96;put()&#96;) exercises the &#96;persisted=True&#96; branch of &#96;_validate_store_invariants&#96; so the &#96;load_chain()&#96;-side completion-invariant rejection path has direct coverage independent of &#96;put()&#96;.
- Statusbegründung: Verified fixed and re-checked against current source in this final review round; covered by bound attestation validation-ca6854031cff (775 passed/0 failed).

### `C-04` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_structured_baseline()&#96; (invoked unconditionally, uncaught, from every &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call — i.e. every rebind/resume within a work unit) derives the &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96; idempotency key solely from &#96;unit.work_unit_id&#96; (&#96;idempotency_key=f"work-unit:{unit.work_unit_id}"&#96;), while the payload it constructs embeds &#96;round_number=unit.round_number&#96;. Elsewhere in the same diff (&#96;persist_codex_contract&#96;'s &#96;logical=f"agent-{unit.work_unit_id}-{state.current_step.value}-{unit.round_number}"&#96;, and &#96;persist_review_contract&#96;'s explicit &#96;round_number&#96; parameter used in its own idempotency key) the codebase treats &#96;round_number&#96; as a value that legitimately changes across multiple rounds within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96; (repair/retry/second-review cycles). The first &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call for round 1 durably publishes &#96;WorkUnitPayload(round_number=1,...)&#96; under key &#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;&#96;. As soon as the same work unit advances to round 2 and &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; fires again, &#96;ArtifactBridge.append()&#96; finds the existing record under the identical idempotency key, but &#96;_digest(record.payload) != _digest(new_payload)&#96; (round_number differs) trips &#96;_assert_equal()&#96;, raising &#96;ArtifactBridgeError&#96;. &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96; is called directly, not through &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96;'s try/except-to-&#96;WorkflowExecutionError&#96; wrapper, so this exception is not fail-closed gracefully — it propagates as a raw, undocumented crash out of &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96;, which is the very first thing the driver does on every resume. This is a landmine that fires on essentially any structured-v1-bound slice needing more than one Codex/review round, not merely a rare edge case. The only existing coverage (&#96;test_structured_bind_persists_contract_and_active_work_unit_once&#96;) calls &#96;bind_work_unit(state)&#96; twice with the SAME unchanged &#96;state&#96; object, so it cannot and does not exercise a round-number transition within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96;; the defect is unguarded by any test.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_structured_bind_survives_round_number_increase_within_same_work_unit","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Verified fixed and re-checked against current source in this final review round; covered by bound attestation validation-ca6854031cff (775 passed/0 failed).

### `C-14` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: No production code path ever appends a &#96;QuotaPausePayload&#96; record. &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver&#96; (src/orchestrator.py) defines &#96;persist_{codex_contract,review_contract,contract_diagnostic,validation_attestation,validation_request,gate_decision,implementation_handoff}&#96; and &#96;_persist_structured_baseline&#96; (task/work-unit/plan/completion only); &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96; (src/workflow.py) only invokes &#96;persist_gate_decision&#96;, &#96;persist_contract_diagnostic&#96;, &#96;persist_codex_contract&#96;, &#96;persist_review_contract&#96;, &#96;persist_validation_attestation&#96;, &#96;persist_validation_request&#96;. None persists a quota pause. Meanwhile Slice 04's C-06 fix made &#96;resolve_resume_state()&#96; (src/artifact_migration.py) symmetric: it now raises &#96;ArtifactResumeError&#96; whenever any mirror-side &#96;InvocationFailureRecord&#96; with &#96;diff_fingerprint&#96;/&#96;resume_at_utc&#96; set (an automatic provider-quota wait, a pre-existing capability covered by tests/test_quota_wait.py) has no matching chain &#96;QuotaPausePayload&#96;. Since &#96;_fresh_state()&#96; now unconditionally binds every new run to &#96;structured-v1&#96; (Slice 06 cutover), any real quota/rate-limit pause-and-automatic-resume on a production run will permanently and unrecoverably break both process-restart resume and the in-process &#96;assert_structured_decision_context()&#96; guard invoked before every subsequent &#96;_agent&#96;/&#96;validate&#96;/&#96;commit_slice&#96;/&#96;finalize_audit&#96; call, with no code path able to self-heal the missing record. This is a severe, source-confirmed availability regression on the new mandatory default protocol.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_structured_artifact_regressions.py::test_automatic_quota_pause_persists_matching_chain_record_and_resumes","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Verified: &#96;_persist_structured_baseline&#96; now idempotently persists &#96;QuotaPausePayload&#96; for every automatic quota/rate-limit pause on every checkpoint, &#96;resolve_resume_state&#96;'s C-06 symmetric mirror check now has a real writer, and the exact acceptance test &#96;tests/test_structured_artifact_regressions.py::test_automatic_quota_pause_persists_matching_chain_record_and_resumes&#96; is present and passes in the bound attestation (validation-78d47673c63e, 779 passed/0 failed). No duplicate-record or fail-closed-resume regression found on inspection.

### `C-15` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1

Slice 05's own report explicitly flagged this exact gap as an accepted open risk to be "in den nachfolgenden Slices weiter gehärtet" (hardened in the following Slices, i.e. Slice 06's cutover), but Slice 06/08 never implemented it. Correction-round audit trails therefore permanently lose their finding-attribution record and render as ordinary Slice work units, defeating a stated Slice-01/05 design deliverable and the "declared requirements and acceptance criteria" / "dead transition states" dimensions explicitly called out for this final review. Does not break fail-closed resume (WORK_UNIT-type matching in resolve_resume_state still succeeds on paths/slice/round alone), but is a confirmed, traceable, unfulfilled architecture commitment.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_correction_work_unit_persists_correction_work_unit_payload_with_finding_ids","-v"]

Exact acceptance test &#96;tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_correction_work_unit_persists_correction_work_unit_payload_with_finding_ids&#96; is present and passes in the bound attestation. No dead-code or attribution-loss regression found on inspection.

### `C-16` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_structured_baseline()&#96; binds &#96;PlanPayload.approved_plan_commit&#96; to the volatile &#96;state.current_slice.start_commit&#96; (re-derived and re-appended on every Slice transition via a matching idempotency-key change) instead of the one immutable, reviewed plan-approval commit; &#96;resolve_resume_state()&#96; mirrors the identical bug so fail-closed resume never detects it. This corrupts the semantic contract of the &#96;approved_plan_commit&#96; field and appends one spurious "approved-plan" &#96;PlanPayload&#96; revision per Slice in any multi-slice structured-v1 run — a real, currently-reachable defect (previously only noted as an accepted Slice-03 residual risk, never tracked or fixed).
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_multi_slice_plan_binding_pins_original_approved_commit_not_slice_start","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: The literal defect is fixed and directly demonstrated: &#96;_persist_structured_baseline&#96; and &#96;resolve_resume_state&#96; now both key exclusively off the immutable &#96;state.approved_plan_commit&#96;, decoupled from per-Slice &#96;current_slice.start_commit&#96;. The exact regression test constructs a genuine two-Slice &#96;bind_work_unit&#96; sequence (not a repeated identical-state call, unlike the earlier C-04 gap) and asserts exactly one &#96;PlanPayload&#96; with the original commit and idempotency key &#96;approved-plan:&lt;commit&gt;&#96;. Backward-compatible state-v3 key-set handling (&#96;plan_commit_keys&#96;/&#96;plan_binding_keys&#96;) is added and round-tripped by &#96;test_hardened_task_contract_roundtrips_in_state&#96;. Bound attestation confirms both the targeted test and the full 780-test suite pass.

### `C-17` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: The new resume-side safety check is fail-open, not fail-closed, for any structured-v1 state.json persisted before this correction (i.e. exactly the runs that could have already hit the pre-fix C-16 bug — including, plausibly, this very live multi-slice run's own on-disk state, which went through Slices 1–9 before &#96;approved_plan_commit&#96; existed). &#96;WorkflowState.from_mapping()&#96; sets &#96;approved_plan_commit = None&#96; via &#96;.get()&#96; for any raw JSON lacking the key (the exact shape every pre-fix &#96;state.json&#96; has). &#96;resolve_resume_state()&#96;'s new guard is gated as &#96;if state.planned_slices and state.work_plan_path and state.approved_plan_commit:&#96; — when &#96;approved_plan_commit&#96; is &#96;None&#96;/falsy, the ENTIRE block, including the newly added &#96;len(plans) != 1&#96; check, is skipped rather than raising &#96;ArtifactResumeError&#96;. Symmetrically, &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96;'s gate &#96;state.approved_plan_commit is not None&#96; means no backfill or self-heal ever occurs for such a state either. Net effect: a chain that already contains multiple divergent &#96;approved-plan&#96; &#96;PlanPayload&#96; revisions (the precise corruption C-16 produced) resumes silently and successfully under the corrected code instead of halting for repair, directly contradicting this repository's fail-closed resume policy ("Missing, corrupt, unknown, or mirror-divergent structured records require restoring the matching chain or mirror before continuation") and the repository-wide rule that resume must never silently tolerate a state the run's own invariants were just hardened to reject. No test in this round (only &#96;tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py&#96;, &#96;tests/test_workflow_state.py&#96; were touched) exercises resume of a legacy-shaped &#96;state.json&#96; (no &#96;approved_plan_commit&#96; key) whose chain already has 2+ &#96;PlanPayload&#96; records.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_artifact_migration.py::test_structured_resume_halts_when_legacy_state_missing_approved_plan_commit_but_chain_has_multiple_plan_records","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Verified fixed on inspection of this round's diff: resolve_resume_state() no longer skips validation when approved_plan_commit is falsy — the gate now enters on &#96;planned_slices and work_plan_path&#96; alone and explicitly raises ArtifactResumeError both when more than one PlanPayload record exists (&#96;len(plans) != 1&#96;) and when &#96;state.approved_plan_commit is None&#96;, closing the fail-open path a legacy/pre-fix state.json (and the C-16-corrupted multi-PlanPayload chain it could have produced) previously exploited to resume silently. The dedicated regression test (tests/test_artifact_migration.py::test_structured_resume_halts_when_legacy_state_missing_approved_plan_commit_but_chain_has_multiple_plan_records) constructs exactly that legacy-shaped, no-approved_plan_commit state with two chain PlanPayload records and asserts the expected ArtifactResumeError message; it is not among the 5 failures in the bound attestation, so the specific C-17 defect is fixed independent of the newly discovered C-18 regression. Note for the correction that closes C-18: the same reader-gate change is the direct cause of C-18, so the eventual fix must keep the C-17 fail-closed guarantee for corrupted/legacy IMPLEMENT-mode mirrors while excluding legitimate pre-approval PLAN_ONLY states (e.g. by scoping the check to states where &#96;approved_plan_commit&#96; is expected to already be bound, mirroring the same condition &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96; uses to decide whether to write it, or by keying off execution_mode/whether a slice has actually started) rather than reintroducing the original &#96;current_slice.start_commit&#96; proxy verbatim.

### `C-18` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 2
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: The bound orchestrator attestation for the exact fingerprint under review (c6af9f5e2f010502f1a2042df3e0627bc37d9c097db41b2f73710ea815daae5a) is FAIL: 5 of 781 tests fail, all in tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py and all in the PLAN_ONLY handoff/generation family — test_plan_only_uses_internal_plan_validation_and_commits_no_product_code, test_plan_only_retries_non_handoff_plan_once_then_halts_before_review, test_plan_only_repairs_handoff_contract_before_review, test_generated_implementation_handoff_skips_second_plan_review, test_completed_plan_resume_retries_failed_handoff_without_agents. This correction round's own C-16/C-17 fix in src/artifact_migration.py::resolve_resume_state() widened the plan-binding resume gate from &#96;if state.planned_slices and state.work_plan_path and state.current_slice.start_commit:&#96; to &#96;if state.planned_slices and state.work_plan_path:&#96;, dropping the &#96;current_slice.start_commit&#96; condition that previously scoped this check away from early/PLAN_ONLY lifecycle states. The gate body now unconditionally raises ArtifactResumeError/mismatch whenever &#96;state.approved_plan_commit is None&#96; (&#96;"state-v3 mirror is missing its approved-plan commit binding"&#96;) as soon as &#96;planned_slices&#96;+&#96;work_plan_path&#96; are set, with no distinction between (a) a genuinely corrupted/legacy IMPLEMENT-mode mirror (the case C-17 targets) and (b) a PLAN_ONLY run's own in-progress internal plan-validation/handoff-generation state, where &#96;work_plan_path&#96;/&#96;planned_slices&#96; can legitimately be populated before an &#96;approved_plan_commit&#96; exists (the plan has not yet been reviewed/approved). &#96;_fresh_state()&#96;'s new unconditional &#96;approved_plan_commit=task_contract.approved_plan_commit&#96; binding and &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96;'s matching &#96;state.approved_plan_commit is not None&#96; writer gate are not symmetric with this widened reader gate for the PLAN_ONLY code path, which is exactly the untested combination this round introduced (only tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_multi_slice_plan_binding_pins_original_approved_commit_not_slice_start and tests/test_artifact_migration.py's new legacy-state test were added; no PLAN_ONLY-path regression test was run before this diff was submitted). This is a genuine, orchestrator-confirmed availability regression in the PLAN_ONLY internal validation/handoff-repair/resume flow, not a pre-existing failure — it must be fixed in the scope of this same correction Slice; it must not merge red, and no red-state follow-up Slice is authorized for this fingerprint.

- Statusbegründung: The exact acceptance test and the full 781-test suite pass under the bound attestation for this fingerprint; the reader-side gate is now correctly scoped to &#96;execution_mode == "IMPLEMENT"&#96;, restoring PLAN_ONLY resume while preserving the C-17 fail-closed guarantee for corrupted/legacy IMPLEMENT-mode mirrors (verified via the retained legacy-state regression test). Writer/reader remain symmetric for the real IMPLEMENT path since only IMPLEMENT-mode task contracts carry &#96;approved_plan_commit&#96; at state creation.

### `C-03` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `OBSERVATION`
- Finding: &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; can raise an exception (e.g., a transient error during the mandatory post-publish re-scan/head-cache refresh) after the record bytes are already durably and atomically published to &#96;records_dir&#96;, and this "durable-but-reported-as-failed" retry contract is exercised by one crash-recovery test but is not documented as an explicit API guarantee on &#96;put()&#96;. Slice 03 (dual-write/semantic equality) will depend on exactly this guarantee to decide whether to retry vs. treat an exception as "nothing was written," so an undocumented/underspecified contract here risks a double-write or a lost-write assumption once callers are wired in.
- Akzeptanztest: Add a docstring/contract note on &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; stating that any exception raised after bytes are written to &#96;records_dir&#96; must be treated as "possibly already persisted, safe to retry the identical call," and add a scan-time test that directly (via a hand-written envelope, bypassing &#96;put()&#96;) exercises the &#96;persisted=True&#96; branch of &#96;_validate_store_invariants&#96; so the &#96;load_chain()&#96;-side completion-invariant rejection path has direct coverage independent of &#96;put()&#96;.
- Statusbegründung: Re-verified against current source under bound attestation validation-1fa8fbf6f047; fix present and matches original closure rationale.

### `C-04` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_structured_baseline()&#96; (invoked unconditionally, uncaught, from every &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call — i.e. every rebind/resume within a work unit) derives the &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96; idempotency key solely from &#96;unit.work_unit_id&#96; (&#96;idempotency_key=f"work-unit:{unit.work_unit_id}"&#96;), while the payload it constructs embeds &#96;round_number=unit.round_number&#96;. Elsewhere in the same diff (&#96;persist_codex_contract&#96;'s &#96;logical=f"agent-{unit.work_unit_id}-{state.current_step.value}-{unit.round_number}"&#96;, and &#96;persist_review_contract&#96;'s explicit &#96;round_number&#96; parameter used in its own idempotency key) the codebase treats &#96;round_number&#96; as a value that legitimately changes across multiple rounds within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96; (repair/retry/second-review cycles). The first &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call for round 1 durably publishes &#96;WorkUnitPayload(round_number=1,...)&#96; under key &#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;&#96;. As soon as the same work unit advances to round 2 and &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; fires again, &#96;ArtifactBridge.append()&#96; finds the existing record under the identical idempotency key, but &#96;_digest(record.payload) != _digest(new_payload)&#96; (round_number differs) trips &#96;_assert_equal()&#96;, raising &#96;ArtifactBridgeError&#96;. &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96; is called directly, not through &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96;'s try/except-to-&#96;WorkflowExecutionError&#96; wrapper, so this exception is not fail-closed gracefully — it propagates as a raw, undocumented crash out of &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96;, which is the very first thing the driver does on every resume. This is a landmine that fires on essentially any structured-v1-bound slice needing more than one Codex/review round, not merely a rare edge case. The only existing coverage (&#96;test_structured_bind_persists_contract_and_active_work_unit_once&#96;) calls &#96;bind_work_unit(state)&#96; twice with the SAME unchanged &#96;state&#96; object, so it cannot and does not exercise a round-number transition within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96;; the defect is unguarded by any test.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_structured_bind_survives_round_number_increase_within_same_work_unit","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Re-verified against current source under bound attestation validation-1fa8fbf6f047; fix present and matches original closure rationale.

### `C-05` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1

Worse: if a round's implementation is superseded (its validation fails and the work unit advances to a new round/fingerprint without ever producing a &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; for the failed round's exact fingerprint — a normal repair/retry path this same diff series already treats as legitimate, e.g. C-04/C-05/C-06), that round's Gate/Validation/Binding records can never gain a matching fingerprint and are permanently excluded from the Slice's own audit trail, even after the Slice is later approved. ### `C-14` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1

None persists a quota pause. Meanwhile Slice 04's C-06 fix made &#96;resolve_resume_state()&#96; (src/artifact_migration.py) symmetric: it now raises &#96;ArtifactResumeError&#96; whenever any mirror-side &#96;InvocationFailureRecord&#96; with &#96;diff_fingerprint&#96;/&#96;resume_at_utc&#96; set (an automatic provider-quota wait, a pre-existing capability covered by tests/test_quota_wait.py) has no matching chain &#96;QuotaPausePayload&#96;. Since &#96;_fresh_state()&#96; now unconditionally binds every new run to &#96;structured-v1&#96; (Slice 06 cutover), any real quota/rate-limit pause-and-automatic-resume on a production run will permanently and unrecoverably break both process-restart resume and the in-process &#96;assert_structured_decision_context()&#96; guard invoked before every subsequent &#96;_agent&#96;/&#96;validate&#96;/&#96;commit_slice&#96;/&#96;finalize_audit&#96; call, with no code path able to self-heal the missing record. This is a severe, source-confirmed availability regression on the new mandatory default protocol.

### `C-17` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: The new resume-side safety check is fail-open, not fail-closed, for any structured-v1 state.json persisted before this correction (i.e. exactly the runs that could have already hit the pre-fix C-16 bug — including, plausibly, this very live multi-slice run's own on-disk state, which went through Slices 1–9 before &#96;approved_plan_commit&#96; existed). &#96;WorkflowState.from_mapping()&#96; sets &#96;approved_plan_commit = None&#96; via &#96;.get()&#96; for any raw JSON lacking the key (the exact shape every pre-fix &#96;state.json&#96; has). &#96;resolve_resume_state()&#96;'s new guard is gated as &#96;if state.planned_slices and state.work_plan_path and state.approved_plan_commit:&#96; — when &#96;approved_plan_commit&#96; is &#96;None&#96;/falsy, the ENTIRE block, including the newly added &#96;len(plans) != 1&#96; check, is skipped rather than raising &#96;ArtifactResumeError&#96;. Symmetrically, &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96;'s gate &#96;state.approved_plan_commit is not None&#96; means no backfill or self-heal ever occurs for such a state either. Net effect: a chain that already contains multiple divergent &#96;approved-plan&#96; &#96;PlanPayload&#96; revisions (the precise corruption C-16 produced) resumes silently and successfully under the corrected code instead of halting for repair, directly contradicting this repository's fail-closed resume policy ("Missing, corrupt, unknown, or mirror-divergent structured records require restoring the matching chain or mirror before continuation") and the repository-wide rule that resume must never silently tolerate a state the run's own invariants were just hardened to reject. No test in this round (only &#96;tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py&#96;, &#96;tests/test_workflow_state.py&#96; were touched) exercises resume of a legacy-shaped &#96;state.json&#96; (no &#96;approved_plan_commit&#96; key) whose chain already has 2+ &#96;PlanPayload&#96; records.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_artifact_migration.py::test_structured_resume_halts_when_legacy_state_missing_approved_plan_commit_but_chain_has_multiple_plan_records","-v"]

This is a genuine, orchestrator-confirmed availability regression in the PLAN_ONLY internal validation/handoff-repair/resume flow, not a pre-existing failure — it must be fixed in the scope of this same correction Slice; it must not merge red, and no red-state follow-up Slice is authorized for this fingerprint.

### `C-04` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_structured_baseline()&#96; (invoked unconditionally, uncaught, from every &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call — i.e. every rebind/resume within a work unit) derives the &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96; idempotency key solely from &#96;unit.work_unit_id&#96; (&#96;idempotency_key=f"work-unit:{unit.work_unit_id}"&#96;), while the payload it constructs embeds &#96;round_number=unit.round_number&#96;. Elsewhere in the same diff (&#96;persist_codex_contract&#96;'s &#96;logical=f"agent-{unit.work_unit_id}-{state.current_step.value}-{unit.round_number}"&#96;, and &#96;persist_review_contract&#96;'s explicit &#96;round_number&#96; parameter used in its own idempotency key) the codebase treats &#96;round_number&#96; as a value that legitimately changes across multiple rounds within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96; (repair/retry/second-review cycles). The first &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call for round 1 durably publishes &#96;WorkUnitPayload(round_number=1,...)&#96; under key &#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;&#96;. As soon as the same work unit advances to round 2 and &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; fires again, &#96;ArtifactBridge.append()&#96; finds the existing record under the identical idempotency key, but &#96;_digest(record.payload) != _digest(new_payload)&#96; (round_number differs) trips &#96;_assert_equal()&#96;, raising &#96;ArtifactBridgeError&#96;. &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96; is called directly, not through &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96;'s try/except-to-&#96;WorkflowExecutionError&#96; wrapper, so this exception is not fail-closed gracefully — it propagates as a raw, undocumented crash out of &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96;, which is the very first thing the driver does on every resume. This is a landmine that fires on essentially any structured-v1-bound slice needing more than one Codex/review round, not merely a rare edge case. The only existing coverage (&#96;test_structured_bind_persists_contract_and_active_work_unit_once&#96;) calls &#96;bind_work_unit(state)&#96; twice with the SAME unchanged &#96;state&#96; object, so it cannot and does not exercise a round-number transition within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96;; the defect is unguarded by any test.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_structured_bind_survives_round_number_increase_within_same_work_unit","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Symmetric chain↔mirror check for quota pauses verified present; QuotaPausePayload records are cross-checked against the mirrored pause state.

### `C-14` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: No production code path ever appends a &#96;QuotaPausePayload&#96; record. &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver&#96; (src/orchestrator.py) defines &#96;persist_{codex_contract,review_contract,contract_diagnostic,validation_attestation,validation_request,gate_decision,implementation_handoff}&#96; and &#96;_persist_structured_baseline&#96; (task/work-unit/plan/completion only); &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96; (src/workflow.py) only invokes &#96;persist_gate_decision&#96;, &#96;persist_contract_diagnostic&#96;, &#96;persist_codex_contract&#96;, &#96;persist_review_contract&#96;, &#96;persist_validation_attestation&#96;, &#96;persist_validation_request&#96;. None persists a quota pause. Meanwhile Slice 04's C-06 fix made &#96;resolve_resume_state()&#96; (src/artifact_migration.py) symmetric: it now raises &#96;ArtifactResumeError&#96; whenever any mirror-side &#96;InvocationFailureRecord&#96; with &#96;diff_fingerprint&#96;/&#96;resume_at_utc&#96; set (an automatic provider-quota wait, a pre-existing capability covered by tests/test_quota_wait.py) has no matching chain &#96;QuotaPausePayload&#96;. Since &#96;_fresh_state()&#96; now unconditionally binds every new run to &#96;structured-v1&#96; (Slice 06 cutover), any real quota/rate-limit pause-and-automatic-resume on a production run will permanently and unrecoverably break both process-restart resume and the in-process &#96;assert_structured_decision_context()&#96; guard invoked before every subsequent &#96;_agent&#96;/&#96;validate&#96;/&#96;commit_slice&#96;/&#96;finalize_audit&#96; call, with no code path able to self-heal the missing record. This is a severe, source-confirmed availability regression on the new mandatory default protocol.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_structured_artifact_regressions.py::test_automatic_quota_pause_persists_matching_chain_record_and_resumes","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Reconfirmed fixed in current diff against original closure rationale; no regression found on re-derivation.

### `C-15` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;CorrectionWorkUnitPayload&#96; (schema + model, with required &#96;finding_ids&#96;) and its dedicated projection rendering ("Korrektur-Work-Unit" branch in artifact_projection.py) are unreachable dead code in production. &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_structured_baseline()&#96; unconditionally persists every non-PLAN work unit — including correction rounds — as a plain &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;, never branching on the correction &#96;WorkUnitKind&#96; to build &#96;CorrectionWorkUnitPayload&#96; with its finding attribution. Slice 05's own report explicitly flagged this exact gap as an accepted open risk to be "in den nachfolgenden Slices weiter gehärtet" (hardened in the following Slices, i.e. Slice 06's cutover), but Slice 06/08 never implemented it. Correction-round audit trails therefore permanently lose their finding-attribution record and render as ordinary Slice work units, defeating a stated Slice-01/05 design deliverable and the "declared requirements and acceptance criteria" / "dead transition states" dimensions explicitly called out for this final review. Does not break fail-closed resume (WORK_UNIT-type matching in resolve_resume_state still succeeds on paths/slice/round alone), but is a confirmed, traceable, unfulfilled architecture commitment.

### `C-16` — `CLOSED`

### `C-17` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: The new resume-side safety check is fail-open, not fail-closed, for any structured-v1 state.json persisted before this correction (i.e. exactly the runs that could have already hit the pre-fix C-16 bug — including, plausibly, this very live multi-slice run's own on-disk state, which went through Slices 1–9 before &#96;approved_plan_commit&#96; existed). &#96;WorkflowState.from_mapping()&#96; sets &#96;approved_plan_commit = None&#96; via &#96;.get()&#96; for any raw JSON lacking the key (the exact shape every pre-fix &#96;state.json&#96; has). &#96;resolve_resume_state()&#96;'s new guard is gated as &#96;if state.planned_slices and state.work_plan_path and state.approved_plan_commit:&#96; — when &#96;approved_plan_commit&#96; is &#96;None&#96;/falsy, the ENTIRE block, including the newly added &#96;len(plans) != 1&#96; check, is skipped rather than raising &#96;ArtifactResumeError&#96;. Symmetrically, &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96;'s gate &#96;state.approved_plan_commit is not None&#96; means no backfill or self-heal ever occurs for such a state either. Net effect: a chain that already contains multiple divergent &#96;approved-plan&#96; &#96;PlanPayload&#96; revisions (the precise corruption C-16 produced) resumes silently and successfully under the corrected code instead of halting for repair, directly contradicting this repository's fail-closed resume policy ("Missing, corrupt, unknown, or mirror-divergent structured records require restoring the matching chain or mirror before continuation") and the repository-wide rule that resume must never silently tolerate a state the run's own invariants were just hardened to reject. No test in this round (only &#96;tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py&#96;, &#96;tests/test_workflow_state.py&#96; were touched) exercises resume of a legacy-shaped &#96;state.json&#96; (no &#96;approved_plan_commit&#96; key) whose chain already has 2+ &#96;PlanPayload&#96; records.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_artifact_migration.py::test_structured_resume_halts_when_legacy_state_missing_approved_plan_commit_but_chain_has_multiple_plan_records","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Reconfirmed fixed in current diff against original closure rationale; no regression found on re-derivation.

This is a genuine, orchestrator-confirmed availability regression in the PLAN_ONLY internal validation/handoff-repair/resume flow, not a pre-existing failure — it must be fixed in the scope of this same correction Slice; it must not merge red, and no red-state follow-up Slice is authorized for this fingerprint.

This is exactly the fail-open "chain-empty ⇒ check skipped" pattern already fixed as BLOCKER for gate decisions (C-05) and quota pauses (C-06), and is structurally identical to the symmetric-set pattern (&#96;set(x) != set(y)&#96;) used everywhere else in this function (task, attestations, quota, commit bindings) — but was never applied to workflow completion itself. &#96;tests/test_artifact_migration.py&#96; has no test constructing a &#96;state.json&#96; at &#96;WorkflowStep.COMPLETED&#96; with all slices committed but zero chain &#96;WorkflowCompletionPayload&#96; records, so the gap is unguarded.

### `C-03` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `OBSERVATION`
- Finding: &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; can raise an exception (e.g., a transient error during the mandatory post-publish re-scan/head-cache refresh) after the record bytes are already durably and atomically published to &#96;records_dir&#96;, and this "durable-but-reported-as-failed" retry contract is exercised by one crash-recovery test but is not documented as an explicit API guarantee on &#96;put()&#96;. Slice 03 (dual-write/semantic equality) will depend on exactly this guarantee to decide whether to retry vs. treat an exception as "nothing was written," so an undocumented/underspecified contract here risks a double-write or a lost-write assumption once callers are wired in.
- Akzeptanztest: Add a docstring/contract note on &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; stating that any exception raised after bytes are written to &#96;records_dir&#96; must be treated as "possibly already persisted, safe to retry the identical call," and add a scan-time test that directly (via a hand-written envelope, bypassing &#96;put()&#96;) exercises the &#96;persisted=True&#96; branch of &#96;_validate_store_invariants&#96; so the &#96;load_chain()&#96;-side completion-invariant rejection path has direct coverage independent of &#96;put()&#96;.
- Statusbegründung: Symmetric chain↔mirror check for finding transitions verified present; FINDING_STATUS/FINDING_RECLASSIFIED transitions are reconciled against chain records on resume.

### `C-04` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_structured_baseline()&#96; (invoked unconditionally, uncaught, from every &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call — i.e. every rebind/resume within a work unit) derives the &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96; idempotency key solely from &#96;unit.work_unit_id&#96; (&#96;idempotency_key=f"work-unit:{unit.work_unit_id}"&#96;), while the payload it constructs embeds &#96;round_number=unit.round_number&#96;. Elsewhere in the same diff (&#96;persist_codex_contract&#96;'s &#96;logical=f"agent-{unit.work_unit_id}-{state.current_step.value}-{unit.round_number}"&#96;, and &#96;persist_review_contract&#96;'s explicit &#96;round_number&#96; parameter used in its own idempotency key) the codebase treats &#96;round_number&#96; as a value that legitimately changes across multiple rounds within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96; (repair/retry/second-review cycles). The first &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call for round 1 durably publishes &#96;WorkUnitPayload(round_number=1,...)&#96; under key &#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;&#96;. As soon as the same work unit advances to round 2 and &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; fires again, &#96;ArtifactBridge.append()&#96; finds the existing record under the identical idempotency key, but &#96;_digest(record.payload) != _digest(new_payload)&#96; (round_number differs) trips &#96;_assert_equal()&#96;, raising &#96;ArtifactBridgeError&#96;. &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96; is called directly, not through &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96;'s try/except-to-&#96;WorkflowExecutionError&#96; wrapper, so this exception is not fail-closed gracefully — it propagates as a raw, undocumented crash out of &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96;, which is the very first thing the driver does on every resume. This is a landmine that fires on essentially any structured-v1-bound slice needing more than one Codex/review round, not merely a rare edge case. The only existing coverage (&#96;test_structured_bind_persists_contract_and_active_work_unit_once&#96;) calls &#96;bind_work_unit(state)&#96; twice with the SAME unchanged &#96;state&#96; object, so it cannot and does not exercise a round-number transition within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96;; the defect is unguarded by any test.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_structured_bind_survives_round_number_increase_within_same_work_unit","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Symmetric chain↔mirror check for quota pauses verified present; QuotaPausePayload records are cross-checked against the mirrored pause state.

### `C-06` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 2
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;resolve_resume_state()&#96;'s quota-pause verification in &#96;src/artifact_migration.py&#96; checks only the chain→mirror direction (&#96;fact not in quota_facts&#96;) and has no symmetric check that every mirror-side quota-pause fact (&#96;unit.invocation_failures&#96; with &#96;diff_fingerprint&#96;/&#96;resume_at_utc&#96; set) has a matching &#96;QuotaPausePayload&#96; chain record, unlike the gate-decision and finding-transition checks just corrected in this same round using &#96;set(...) != set(...)&#96;. ### `C-09` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 2

This directly contradicts the class docstring just added to close C-08, which states only "task, plan, and finding-transition records remain visible" globally — gates, validation attestations, and commit bindings are not in that list, yet behave identically to findings. &#96;src/orchestrator.py&#96;'s &#96;_project_audit()&#96; now calls &#96;project_structured_slice_audit()&#96; on every checkpoint in structured-v1 mode, so every Slice's persisted Markdown document will accumulate every other Slice's validation attestations, test-change gates, and commit/handoff bindings, unboundedly, for the life of the run — an unscoped leak of unrelated Slices' operational detail into every Slice's own audit trail, the exact failure mode C-08 was raised (and just closed) to prevent, but for three payload types the fix never covers. No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_projection.py&#96; exercises Slice-exclusion for &#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, or &#96;BindingPayload&#96;; the only new regression test (&#96;test_slice_projection_accepts_chain_subsequence_and_excludes_other_work_unit&#96;) proves scoping solely for &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;ReviewPayload&#96; and intentional global scope solely for &#96;FindingTransitionPayload&#96;.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_artifact_projection.py::test_slice_projection_excludes_other_slice_gate_validation_and_binding_records","-v"]

### `C-10` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 3
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: The C-09 fix correlates ownerless payload types (&#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;, &#96;BindingPayload&#96;) to a Slice exclusively via &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96;, and &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96; is populated only from records whose payload exposes a &#96;work_unit_id&#96; attribute — across every payload type in &#96;artifact_models&#96;/&#96;artifact_projection&#96;, that is &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; alone (&#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;CorrectionWorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;GatePayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;/&#96;BindingPayload&#96;/&#96;TaskPayload&#96;/&#96;PlanPayload&#96;/&#96;FindingTransitionPayload&#96; never populate it). Consequently, any Gate/ValidationRequest/ValidationAttestation/Binding record produced for a Slice's own active round is invisible in that Slice's own &#96;render_sections()&#96; / persisted Markdown document until a &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; carrying the identical &#96;record.fingerprint&#96; is later appended for that exact round. &#96;src/orchestrator.py::_project_audit()&#96; calls &#96;project_structured_slice_audit()&#96; on every checkpoint in structured-v1 mode, including immediately after a validation attestation and before the corresponding Claude/Antigravity review is recorded — at that checkpoint the very validation-attestation/gate records a reviewer needs are missing from the persisted Slice document, undermining exactly the "record-native view" this Slice exists to deliver and that Slice 06's cutover is meant to make authoritative. Worse: if a round's implementation is superseded (its validation fails and the work unit advances to a new round/fingerprint without ever producing a &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; for the failed round's exact fingerprint — a normal repair/retry path this same diff series already treats as legitimate, e.g. C-04/C-05/C-06), that round's Gate/Validation/Binding records can never gain a matching fingerprint and are permanently excluded from the Slice's own audit trail, even after the Slice is later approved. No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_projection.py&#96; exercises a Gate/ValidationAttestation/Binding record for the Slice's own round before a matching-fingerprint &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; exists; the only new checkpoint-level test (&#96;test_structured_checkpoint_projects_record_chain_into_slice_and_overall_audits&#96;) only asserts a generic digest heading and a scope path, not this section's content, so the gap is unguarded.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_artifact_projection.py::test_slice_projection_includes_own_round_gate_and_validation_records_before_first_review","-v"]

### `C-14` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: No production code path ever appends a &#96;QuotaPausePayload&#96; record. &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver&#96; (src/orchestrator.py) defines &#96;persist_{codex_contract,review_contract,contract_diagnostic,validation_attestation,validation_request,gate_decision,implementation_handoff}&#96; and &#96;_persist_structured_baseline&#96; (task/work-unit/plan/completion only); &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96; (src/workflow.py) only invokes &#96;persist_gate_decision&#96;, &#96;persist_contract_diagnostic&#96;, &#96;persist_codex_contract&#96;, &#96;persist_review_contract&#96;, &#96;persist_validation_attestation&#96;, &#96;persist_validation_request&#96;. None persists a quota pause. Meanwhile Slice 04's C-06 fix made &#96;resolve_resume_state()&#96; (src/artifact_migration.py) symmetric: it now raises &#96;ArtifactResumeError&#96; whenever any mirror-side &#96;InvocationFailureRecord&#96; with &#96;diff_fingerprint&#96;/&#96;resume_at_utc&#96; set (an automatic provider-quota wait, a pre-existing capability covered by tests/test_quota_wait.py) has no matching chain &#96;QuotaPausePayload&#96;. Since &#96;_fresh_state()&#96; now unconditionally binds every new run to &#96;structured-v1&#96; (Slice 06 cutover), any real quota/rate-limit pause-and-automatic-resume on a production run will permanently and unrecoverably break both process-restart resume and the in-process &#96;assert_structured_decision_context()&#96; guard invoked before every subsequent &#96;_agent&#96;/&#96;validate&#96;/&#96;commit_slice&#96;/&#96;finalize_audit&#96; call, with no code path able to self-heal the missing record. This is a severe, source-confirmed availability regression on the new mandatory default protocol.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_structured_artifact_regressions.py::test_automatic_quota_pause_persists_matching_chain_record_and_resumes","-v"]

### `C-17` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: The new resume-side safety check is fail-open, not fail-closed, for any structured-v1 state.json persisted before this correction (i.e. exactly the runs that could have already hit the pre-fix C-16 bug — including, plausibly, this very live multi-slice run's own on-disk state, which went through Slices 1–9 before &#96;approved_plan_commit&#96; existed). &#96;WorkflowState.from_mapping()&#96; sets &#96;approved_plan_commit = None&#96; via &#96;.get()&#96; for any raw JSON lacking the key (the exact shape every pre-fix &#96;state.json&#96; has). &#96;resolve_resume_state()&#96;'s new guard is gated as &#96;if state.planned_slices and state.work_plan_path and state.approved_plan_commit:&#96; — when &#96;approved_plan_commit&#96; is &#96;None&#96;/falsy, the ENTIRE block, including the newly added &#96;len(plans) != 1&#96; check, is skipped rather than raising &#96;ArtifactResumeError&#96;. Symmetrically, &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96;'s gate &#96;state.approved_plan_commit is not None&#96; means no backfill or self-heal ever occurs for such a state either. Net effect: a chain that already contains multiple divergent &#96;approved-plan&#96; &#96;PlanPayload&#96; revisions (the precise corruption C-16 produced) resumes silently and successfully under the corrected code instead of halting for repair, directly contradicting this repository's fail-closed resume policy ("Missing, corrupt, unknown, or mirror-divergent structured records require restoring the matching chain or mirror before continuation") and the repository-wide rule that resume must never silently tolerate a state the run's own invariants were just hardened to reject. No test in this round (only &#96;tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py&#96;, &#96;tests/test_workflow_state.py&#96; were touched) exercises resume of a legacy-shaped &#96;state.json&#96; (no &#96;approved_plan_commit&#96; key) whose chain already has 2+ &#96;PlanPayload&#96; records.

These exercise ordinary structured-v1 plan/commit/gate/resume sequences where the mirror can legitimately reach &#96;WorkflowStep.COMPLETED&#96; with every slice's &#96;commit_ref&#96; set at a point that precedes or is decoupled from the actual &#96;WorkflowCompletionPayload&#96; chain append, so the new guard cannot distinguish this legitimate in-flight production ordering from the genuinely corrupted/mismatched-restore mirror state C-19 targets — the same reader-gate-too-broad failure pattern previously seen and fixed in C-17/C-18 for the plan-binding fact, now reintroduced for the completion fact. This must be fixed within the current correction Slice's own scope (&#96;src/artifact_migration.py&#96;, and &#96;src/orchestrator.py&#96;/&#96;src/workflow.py&#96; if the actual defect is completion-record persist ordering rather than the resume-check itself, all already in this Slice's allowlist); it must not merge while the bound attestation is red, and no red-state follow-up Slice is authorized for this fingerprint.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_inbox_workflow_creates_slice_audit_at_implementation_start_and_finalizes_overall","tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_production_session_plans_commits_two_slices_and_persists_final_state","tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_not_ready_gate_resumes_with_plain_resume_without_explicit_approval","tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_generated_implementation_handoff_skips_second_plan_review","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Reconfirmed fixed in current diff against original closure rationale; no regression found on re-derivation.

### `C-21` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 2
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: &#96;resolve_resume_state()&#96;'s commit-binding check in &#96;src/artifact_migration.py&#96; (and the analogous plan_commit/implementation_handoff paths reachable from &#96;persist_implementation_handoff&#96;/&#96;commit_slice&#96; in &#96;src/orchestrator.py&#96;) verifies only that &#96;BindingPayload.target&#96; is a member of &#96;state.slices[*].commit_ref&#96; — it never verifies that &#96;BindingPayload.attestation_id&#96; resolves to an actually-persisted &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96; record sharing the binding's fingerprint, nor that every entry of &#96;BindingPayload.approval_ids&#96; resolves to an actually-persisted &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; record with &#96;verdict == "approved"&#96; and the same fingerprint. &#96;BindingPayload.__post_init__&#96; in &#96;src/artifact_models.py&#96; only calls &#96;_require_identifier&#96;/&#96;_require_unique_identifiers&#96; (generic string-shape checks); &#96;ArtifactStore._validate_store_invariants&#96; performs no cross-record existence/type check either. &#96;assert_structured_decision_context()&#96; in &#96;src/orchestrator.py&#96; reconciles &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; records against the state-v3 mirror as an unordered &#96;Counter&#96; over the whole run, not scoped to the specific attestation/approval set a given commit binding actually cites. This is exactly the "fail-open chain-empty/unreferenced ⇒ silently accepted" pattern already found and fixed as BLOCKER in this same branch for gate decisions (C-05), quota pauses (C-06), the approved-plan binding (C-16/C-17), and workflow completion (C-19) — but it was never extended to the commit/plan_commit/implementation_handoff &#96;BindingPayload&#96; records themselves, which are the terminal authorization artifact resume relies on to prove a committed Slice or handoff was genuinely reviewed and validated. A restored/corrupted record directory (mismatched backup restore, truncated/hand-edited records) containing a schema-valid commit &#96;BindingPayload&#96; whose &#96;attestation_id&#96;/&#96;approval_ids&#96; point to nonexistent or unrelated records is therefore accepted as a valid &#96;structured-v1&#96; resume instead of halting with &#96;ArtifactResumeError&#96;, directly contradicting the repository's fail-closed resume contract ("Missing, corrupt, unknown, or mirror-divergent structured records require restoring the matching chain or mirror before continuation"). No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_migration.py&#96; constructs a chain whose commit &#96;BindingPayload.attestation_id&#96; or &#96;approval_ids&#96; do not resolve to real, fingerprint-matching records; this defect is unguarded by any test across the 11 correction rounds reviewed above, which otherwise systematically closed every other instance of this exact asymmetry class.
- Akzeptanztest: acceptance=VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_artifact_migration.py::test_structured_resume_halts_when_commit_binding_references_unknown_or_mismatched_attestation_or_approval","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: Symmetric attestation/approval referential-integrity check for BindingPayload verified present and covered by the exact required parametrized regression test; provenance can no longer be fabricated across a restored/corrupted record chain.

### `C-22` — `CLOSED`

- Quelle: `claude`; Runde 1
- Klasse: `BLOCKER`
- Finding: src/orchestrator.py's &#96;_recover_completed_reviewer_contract()&#96; had its &#96;AgentFailureKind.AUTH&#96;-only gate removed in this correction round (unrelated to and unrequired by C-21), so it now recovers a "complete reviewer contract" from ANY misclassified &#96;AgentInvocationError&#96;, including &#96;AgentFailureKind.QUOTA&#96; — the exact classification that must otherwise route through the quota-pause/resume machinery this branch already hardened (C-06/C-14) to guarantee a matching &#96;QuotaPausePayload&#96; chain record for every automatic quota wait. No test in this diff proves that a QUOTA-classified failure whose &#96;provider_text&#96; parses as a complete contract still results in the correct quota-pause record being persisted (or is intentionally exempt with a documented rationale); as written, such an event is silently treated as a successful review, bypassing the provider-quota wait window and producing an audit trail that misrepresents what actually happened. This is an unrequested, undocumented scope widening inside a correction-convergence round and reopens the exact bypass class C-06/C-14 were raised to close, for a new, untested code path.
- Akzeptanztest: VALIDATE: ["python3","-m","pytest","tests/test_orchestrator_runtime.py::test_quota_classified_recoverable_contract_still_persists_matching_quota_pause_record","-v"]
- Statusbegründung: The reported scope-widening bug (AUTH-only recovery gate silently accepting QUOTA-classified failures) is closed by direct test evidence under the bound fingerprint: (1) &#96;test_reviewer_does_not_recover_real_incomplete_or_foreign_failure&#96;'s new QUOTA-classified cases — including one whose &#96;provider_text&#96; is a fully valid, complete reviewer contract (&#96;SLICE_APPROVAL: 11 &#124; YES&#96; / &#96;STATUS: DONE&#96;) — assert &#96;caught.value is failure&#96;, i.e. the original AgentInvocationError propagates unrecovered instead of being silently treated as a successful review; (2) &#96;test_quota_classified_recoverable_contract_still_persists_matching_quota_pause_record&#96; drives the real &#96;WorkflowEngine._invoke_role&#96; path with a QUOTA error carrying a complete-looking contract and asserts &#96;calls == 2&#96; (the agent was actually re-invoked rather than short-circuited by recovery), &#96;invocation_failures[-1].automatic_resume is True&#96;, &#96;failure_kind is AgentFailureKind.QUOTA&#96;, and exactly one matching &#96;QuotaPausePayload&#96; chain record with the correct role/retry_at. This is precisely the C-06/C-14 bypass-class guarantee C-22 flagged as reopened, and it is now proven end-to-end (wait → retry → matching chain record), not just at the classification-gate level. I could not see a direct diff hunk for &#96;src/orchestrator.py&#96;'s &#96;_recover_completed_reviewer_contract()&#96; in the supplied packet (only artifact_migration.py/tests/docs hunks were shown), but the acceptance test named in my own finding is the exact test that now passes under the bound attestation, and the negative parametrized cases independently corroborate the behavior at the unit level, so I treat this as sufficient closure evidence per the finding's own acceptance criterion.

Closing as originally reported by me. |
| C-04 | claude | &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_structured_baseline()&#96; (invoked unconditionally, uncaught, from every &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call — i.e. every rebind/resume within a work unit) derives the &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96; idempotency key solely from &#96;unit.work_unit_id&#96; (&#96;idempotency_key=f"work-unit:{unit.work_unit_id}"&#96;), while the payload it constructs embeds &#96;round_number=unit.round_number&#96;. Elsewhere in the same diff (&#96;persist_codex_contract&#96;'s &#96;logical=f"agent-{unit.work_unit_id}-{state.current_step.value}-{unit.round_number}"&#96;, and &#96;persist_review_contract&#96;'s explicit &#96;round_number&#96; parameter used in its own idempotency key) the codebase treats &#96;round_number&#96; as a value that legitimately changes across multiple rounds within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96; (repair/retry/second-review cycles). The first &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call for round 1 durably publishes &#96;WorkUnitPayload(round_number=1,...)&#96; under key &#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;&#96;. As soon as the same work unit advances to round 2 and &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; fires again, &#96;ArtifactBridge.append()&#96; finds the existing record under the identical idempotency key, but &#96;_digest(record.payload) != _digest(new_payload)&#96; (round_number differs) trips &#96;_assert_equal()&#96;, raising &#96;ArtifactBridgeError&#96;. &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96; is called directly, not through &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96;'s try/except-to-&#96;WorkflowExecutionError&#96; wrapper, so this exception is not fail-closed gracefully — it propagates as a raw, undocumented crash out of &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96;, which is the very first thing the driver does on every resume. This is a landmine that fires on essentially any structured-v1-bound slice needing more than one Codex/review round, not merely a rare edge case. The only existing coverage (&#96;test_structured_bind_persists_contract_and_active_work_unit_once&#96;) calls &#96;bind_work_unit(state)&#96; twice with the SAME unchanged &#96;state&#96; object, so it cannot and does not exercise a round-number transition within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96;; the defect is unguarded by any test. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: Idempotency key for WorkUnitPayload now includes round_number (&#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;:round:&lt;n&gt;&#96;), so same-round re-binds stay idempotent and round transitions persist as a new ordered revision instead of raising ArtifactBridgeError. The exact acceptance test (test_structured_bind_survives_round_number_increase_within_same_work_unit) is present, exercises exactly the previously-broken transition, and passes in the bound 744-test attestation for the current fingerprint. Closing as originally reported by me. |

The first &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call for round 1 durably publishes &#96;WorkUnitPayload(round_number=1,...)&#96; under key &#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;&#96;. As soon as the same work unit advances to round 2 and &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; fires again, &#96;ArtifactBridge.append()&#96; finds the existing record under the identical idempotency key, but &#96;_digest(record.payload) != _digest(new_payload)&#96; (round_number differs) trips &#96;_assert_equal()&#96;, raising &#96;ArtifactBridgeError&#96;. &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96; is called directly, not through &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96;'s try/except-to-&#96;WorkflowExecutionError&#96; wrapper, so this exception is not fail-closed gracefully — it propagates as a raw, undocumented crash out of &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96;, which is the very first thing the driver does on every resume. Consequently, any Gate/ValidationRequest/ValidationAttestation/Binding record produced for a Slice's own active round is invisible in that Slice's own &#96;render_sections()&#96; / persisted Markdown document until a &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; carrying the identical &#96;record.fingerprint&#96; is later appended for that exact round. &#96;src/orchestrator.py::_project_audit()&#96; calls &#96;project_structured_slice_audit()&#96; on every checkpoint in structured-v1 mode, including immediately after a validation attestation and before the corresponding Claude/Antigravity review is recorded — at that checkpoint the very validation-attestation/gate records a reviewer needs are missing from the persisted Slice document, undermining exactly the "record-native view" this Slice exists to deliver and that Slice 06's cutover is meant to make authoritative. Worse: if a round's implementation is superseded (its validation fails and the work unit advances to a new round/fingerprint without ever producing a &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; for the failed round's exact fingerprint — a normal repair/retry path this same diff series already treats as legitimate, e.g. C-04/C-05/C-06), that round's Gate/Validation/Binding records can never gain a matching fingerprint and are permanently excluded from the Slice's own audit trail, even after the Slice is later approved. Exact acceptance test present and passing in the bound full-suite attestation (766 passed) for fingerprint 615044a6b8d702f824be3d0d81c2213e6d8a49b933ff962885580dc2a86e10f5; existing C-09 cross-Slice exclusion tests remain green, confirming no regression. Closing as originally reported by me. |

| C-03 | claude | &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; can raise an exception (e.g., a transient error during the mandatory post-publish re-scan/head-cache refresh) after the record bytes are already durably and atomically published to &#96;records_dir&#96;, and this "durable-but-reported-as-failed" retry contract is exercised by one crash-recovery test but is not documented as an explicit API guarantee on &#96;put()&#96;. Slice 03 (dual-write/semantic equality) will depend on exactly this guarantee to decide whether to retry vs. treat an exception as "nothing was written," so an undocumented/underspecified contract here risks a double-write or a lost-write assumption once callers are wired in. | OBSERVATION | angenommen | erledigt: Acceptance criteria fully met in this diff: &#96;ArtifactStore.put()&#96; now carries an explicit docstring contract ("An exception does not prove that nothing was written... callers must treat every exception as possibly already persisted and may safely retry the identical logical call"), and &#96;tests/test_artifact_store.py::test_scan_rejects_persisted_non_success_completion_with_binding&#96; directly exercises the &#96;persisted=True&#96; scan branch via a hand-written envelope that bypasses &#96;put()&#96;, matching my Slice-02 acceptance test verbatim. Closing as originally reported by me. |
| C-04 | claude | &#96;ProductionWorkflowDriver._persist_structured_baseline()&#96; (invoked unconditionally, uncaught, from every &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call — i.e. every rebind/resume within a work unit) derives the &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96; idempotency key solely from &#96;unit.work_unit_id&#96; (&#96;idempotency_key=f"work-unit:{unit.work_unit_id}"&#96;), while the payload it constructs embeds &#96;round_number=unit.round_number&#96;. Elsewhere in the same diff (&#96;persist_codex_contract&#96;'s &#96;logical=f"agent-{unit.work_unit_id}-{state.current_step.value}-{unit.round_number}"&#96;, and &#96;persist_review_contract&#96;'s explicit &#96;round_number&#96; parameter used in its own idempotency key) the codebase treats &#96;round_number&#96; as a value that legitimately changes across multiple rounds within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96; (repair/retry/second-review cycles). The first &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; call for round 1 durably publishes &#96;WorkUnitPayload(round_number=1,...)&#96; under key &#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;&#96;. As soon as the same work unit advances to round 2 and &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96; fires again, &#96;ArtifactBridge.append()&#96; finds the existing record under the identical idempotency key, but &#96;_digest(record.payload) != _digest(new_payload)&#96; (round_number differs) trips &#96;_assert_equal()&#96;, raising &#96;ArtifactBridgeError&#96;. &#96;_persist_structured_baseline()&#96; is called directly, not through &#96;WorkflowEngine._persist_structured&#96;'s try/except-to-&#96;WorkflowExecutionError&#96; wrapper, so this exception is not fail-closed gracefully — it propagates as a raw, undocumented crash out of &#96;bind_work_unit()&#96;, which is the very first thing the driver does on every resume. This is a landmine that fires on essentially any structured-v1-bound slice needing more than one Codex/review round, not merely a rare edge case. The only existing coverage (&#96;test_structured_bind_persists_contract_and_active_work_unit_once&#96;) calls &#96;bind_work_unit(state)&#96; twice with the SAME unchanged &#96;state&#96; object, so it cannot and does not exercise a round-number transition within one &#96;work_unit_id&#96;; the defect is unguarded by any test. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: Idempotency key for WorkUnitPayload now includes round_number (&#96;work-unit:&lt;id&gt;:round:&lt;n&gt;&#96;), so same-round re-binds stay idempotent and round transitions persist as a new ordered revision instead of raising ArtifactBridgeError. The exact acceptance test (test_structured_bind_survives_round_number_increase_within_same_work_unit) is present, exercises exactly the previously-broken transition, and passes in the bound 744-test attestation for the current fingerprint. Closing as originally reported by me. |
| C-05 | claude | &#96;resolve_resume_state()&#96; in &#96;src/artifact_migration.py&#96; verifies gate decisions and finding-status transitions only in the chain→mirror direction (&#96;if fact not in decisions: raise mismatch(...)&#96;, and the analogous finding-status check), unlike the attestation check (&#96;set(attestation_records) != attestation_facts&#96;) and the commit-binding check (&#96;commit_targets - bound_commit_targets&#96;), which are both symmetric. A state-v3 mirror that has advanced past the append-only record chain for a gate decision or a finding-status transition (the mirror-ahead-of-chain partial-write case this slice's own doc calls out as the central risk) is therefore accepted as a valid structured-v1 resume instead of halting with &#96;ArtifactResumeError&#96;, contradicting the documented contract that "missing... spiegelbare Fakten" must produce a resumable repair halt. No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_migration.py&#96; exercises a mirror-side gate decision or finding transition with no chain counterpart. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: Exact acceptance test (two named regression tests) present and passing in the bound attestation; fix applies the same symmetric-set pattern already used for attestations/commit-bindings to gate decisions and finding transitions, correctly closing the mirror-ahead-of-chain gap I reported. |
| C-06 | claude | &#96;resolve_resume_state()&#96;'s quota-pause verification in &#96;src/artifact_migration.py&#96; checks only the chain→mirror direction (&#96;fact not in quota_facts&#96;) and has no symmetric check that every mirror-side quota-pause fact (&#96;unit.invocation_failures&#96; with &#96;diff_fingerprint&#96;/&#96;resume_at_utc&#96; set) has a matching &#96;QuotaPausePayload&#96; chain record, unlike the gate-decision and finding-transition checks just corrected in this same round using &#96;set(...) != set(...)&#96;. A quota pause durably written to state.json whose corresponding artifact-store append failed or was skipped is silently accepted as a valid structured-v1 resume instead of halting, letting a run bypass its intended provider-quota wait window undetected. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: The asymmetric quota-pause check is now symmetric (&#96;set(quota_records) != quota_facts&#96;), matching the pattern used for gate/finding/attestation/commit-binding checks. The exact acceptance test (&#96;test_structured_resume_halts_when_mirror_quota_pause_has_no_chain_record&#96;) is present and passes in the bound attestation for fingerprint d321fd17411b5d2fa0440f09781614671573f18618e4739f4d5b21f50c1b9f15 (755 passed full suite + isolated 1-test run, both PASS). Closing as originally reported by me. |

| C-08 | claude | &#96;ArtifactAuditProjection.selected_records&#96; in &#96;src/artifact_projection.py&#96; cannot correlate a &#96;FindingTransitionPayload&#96; record with any owning Slice/work unit (the payload carries no &#96;work_unit_id&#96;/&#96;slice_id&#96;), so every finding transition in the run is included in every per-Slice Markdown projection via the unconditional &#96;else&#96; branch, unlike &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;ReviewPayload&#96;, which are correctly scoped. Closing as originally reported by me. |
| C-09 | claude | &#96;ArtifactAuditProjection.selected_records&#96; in &#96;src/artifact_projection.py&#96; scopes only &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;CorrectionWorkUnitPayload&#96; directly by &#96;slice_id&#96;, and any other payload type only via an ad-hoc &#96;getattr(payload, "work_unit_id", None)&#96; correlation to that Slice's own work-unit IDs (used for e.g. &#96;ReviewPayload&#96;). &#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;, and &#96;BindingPayload&#96; — rendered into the &#96;test-approval-premortem&#96;, &#96;validation-attestation&#96;, and &#96;approval-status&#96; sections — carry no &#96;work_unit_id&#96; field at all (none of their render branches in the same file reference &#96;payload.work_unit_id&#96;), so every one of these records unconditionally falls into the catch-all &#96;else: selected.append(record)&#96; branch and is included, unfiltered, in *every* Slice-scoped Markdown projection for the entire run, not only the intentionally global overall/work-plan projection. This directly contradicts the class docstring just added to close C-08, which states only "task, plan, and finding-transition records remain visible" globally — gates, validation attestations, and commit bindings are not in that list, yet behave identically to findings. &#96;src/orchestrator.py&#96;'s &#96;_project_audit()&#96; now calls &#96;project_structured_slice_audit()&#96; on every checkpoint in structured-v1 mode, so every Slice's persisted Markdown document will accumulate every other Slice's validation attestations, test-change gates, and commit/handoff bindings, unboundedly, for the life of the run — an unscoped leak of unrelated Slices' operational detail into every Slice's own audit trail, the exact failure mode C-08 was raised (and just closed) to prevent, but for three payload types the fix never covers. No test in &#96;tests/test_artifact_projection.py&#96; exercises Slice-exclusion for &#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, or &#96;BindingPayload&#96;; the only new regression test (&#96;test_slice_projection_accepts_chain_subsequence_and_excludes_other_work_unit&#96;) proves scoping solely for &#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;ReviewPayload&#96; and intentional global scope solely for &#96;FindingTransitionPayload&#96;. | BLOCKER | angenommen | erledigt: The reported cross-slice leak for GatePayload/ValidationRequestPayload/ValidationAttestationPayload/BindingPayload is fixed: &#96;selected_records&#96; now builds &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96; from records whose payload's &#96;work_unit_id&#96; belongs to this slice's own work units, and includes any ownerless-payload record whose &#96;record.fingerprint&#96; is in that set. Hand-traced the new test: slice-6 records (work-unit-13, review-13, validation-request, validation-attestation, gate, binding — all fingerprint &#96;c*64&#96;) are excluded from the &#96;slice_id="5"&#96; projection, while &#96;_chain()&#96;'s own gate/validation/binding (fingerprint &#96;a*64&#96;, linked via that round's &#96;ReviewPayload(work_unit_id="12")&#96;) remain visible. Exact acceptance test I set is present and passes in the bound 765-test attestation. Closing as originally reported by me. |
| C-10 | claude | The C-09 fix correlates ownerless payload types (&#96;GatePayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;, &#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;, &#96;BindingPayload&#96;) to a Slice exclusively via &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96;, and &#96;slice_fingerprints&#96; is populated only from records whose payload exposes a &#96;work_unit_id&#96; attribute — across every payload type in &#96;artifact_models&#96;/&#96;artifact_projection&#96;, that is &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; alone (&#96;WorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;CorrectionWorkUnitPayload&#96;/&#96;GatePayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationRequestPayload&#96;/&#96;ValidationAttestationPayload&#96;/&#96;BindingPayload&#96;/&#96;TaskPayload&#96;/&#96;PlanPayload&#96;/&#96;FindingTransitionPayload&#96; never populate it). Consequently, any Gate/ValidationRequest/ValidationAttestation/Binding record produced for a Slice's own active round is invisible in that Slice's own &#96;render_sections()&#96; / persisted Markdown document until a &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; carrying the identical &#96;record.fingerprint&#96; is later appended for that exact round. &#96;src/orchestrator.py::_project_audit()&#96; calls &#96;project_structured_slice_audit()&#96; on every checkpoint in structured-v1 mode, including immediately after a validation attestation and before the corresponding Claude/Antigravity review is recorded — at that checkpoint the very validation-attestation/gate records a reviewer needs are missing from the persisted Slice document, undermining exactly the "record-native view" this Slice exists to deliver and that Slice 06's cutover is meant to make authoritative. Worse: if a round's implementation is superseded (its validation fails and the work unit advances to a new round/fingerprint without ever producing a &#96;ReviewPayload&#96; for the failed round's exact fingerprint — a normal repair/retry path this same diff series already treats as legitimate, e.g. C-04/C-05/C-06), that round's Gate/Validation/Binding records can never gain a matching fingerprint and are permanently excluded from the Slice's own audit trail, even after the Slice is later approved. Exact acceptance test present and passing in the bound full-suite attestation (766 passed) for fingerprint 615044a6b8d702f824be3d0d81c2213e6d8a49b933ff962885580dc2a86e10f5; existing C-09 cross-Slice exclusion tests remain green, confirming no regression. Closing as originally reported by me. |
| C-11 | claude | The protocol-mode-dependent &#96;ValidationCommand&#96; construction in &#96;_context()&#96; (&#96;argv=tuple(shlex.split(raw_command))&#96; for &#96;STRUCTURED_V1&#96; vs. &#96;shell_command=raw_command&#96; for &#96;LEGACY_STATE_V3&#96;) has no dedicated regression test in this diff and is not mentioned in the Slice-06 report's "Durchgeführte Änderungen"/"Geplante Tests" sections, even though &#96;_fresh_state&#96; now unconditionally binds every new production run to &#96;STRUCTURED_V1&#96;, making this the default path for all future &#96;--test-command&#96; overrides. Untested edge cases include quoted/multi-token &#96;--test-command&#96; values and empty/whitespace-only commands. | OBSERVATION | offen | offen |
| C-12 | claude | &#96;assert_structured_decision_context()&#96; raises &#96;WorkflowExecutionError("structured review mirror is missing its validation binding")&#96; if ANY &#96;ReviewAuditEvent&#96; across ALL persisted work-unit histories for the entire run (not only the active round) has &#96;result.validation is None&#96;; once tripped, every subsequent &#96;_agent&#96;/&#96;validate&#96;/&#96;commit_slice&#96;/&#96;finalize_audit&#96; call for the rest of the run is permanently blocked. No test in the reviewed diff constructs a resumed STRUCTURED_V1 state whose history contains a &#96;STOP_REQUESTED&#96;-style review event to confirm &#96;result.validation&#96; is always populated for stops as well as approvals/denials, so this invariant's completeness rests on an unverified assumption about every reviewer code path. | OBSERVATION | offen | offen |

| C-02 | claude | &#96;WorkflowCompletionPayload&#96;/schema &#96;workflow_completion.allOf&#96; only requires &#96;final_binding_id&#96; to be set when &#96;outcome == "completed"&#96;; it does not forbid a non-null &#96;final_binding_id&#96; for &#96;outcome in {"failed","stopped"}&#96;, permitting a logically inconsistent completion record to pass validation.
